[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)

bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.

---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/release_files.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/release_files.rs
index f7b7a59b5d..93b02aab5d 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/release_files.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/release_files.rs
@@ -175,3 +175,50 @@ impl Drop for Pointer {
         }
     }
 }
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — deletion requires a definitive non-commit, never a lost reply.
+pub(crate) struct Candidate { dir: PathBuf, release: u64, pub committed: bool, pub pending: bool }
+impl Candidate {
+    pub(crate) fn new(dir: &Path, release: u64) -> Self { Self {dir:dir.into(),release,committed:false,pending:false} }
+}
+impl Drop for Candidate {
+    fn drop(&mut self) {
+        if self.committed { return; }
+        if self.pending {eprintln!("release {} result remains unknown; retained source while the server may still commit; inspect releases",self.release);return;}
+        let probe=(|| -> Result<bool,String> {
+            let store=snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&self.dir.join("store.sqlite")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+            let history:bool=store.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE type='table' AND name='_release_events')",[],|r|r.get(0)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
+            if !history {return Ok(false);}
+            store.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _release_events WHERE release=?1 OR previous=?1)",[self.release as i64],|r|r.get(0)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
+        })();
+        match probe {
+            Ok(false)=>if let Err(e)=fs::remove_dir_all(self.dir.join("releases").join(self.release.to_string())) {eprintln!("uncommitted release {} cleanup failed: {e}",self.release);},
+            Ok(true)=>{},
+            Err(e)=>eprintln!("release {} outcome unknown; retained source for recovery; inspect releases: {e}",self.release),
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod candidate_tests {
+    use super::*;
+    #[test]
+    fn cleanup_removes_only_definitively_uncommitted_candidates() {
+        let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-candidate-custody-{}-{}",std::process::id(),snapback4_core::now_ms()));
+        fs::create_dir_all(root.join("releases")).unwrap();
+        struct Cleanup(PathBuf);
+        impl Drop for Cleanup {fn drop(&mut self){let _=fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
+        let _cleanup=Cleanup(root.clone());
+        let store=snapback4_core::Store::open(&root.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
+        store.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TABLE _release_events(release INTEGER,previous INTEGER)").unwrap();
+        for n in 1..=3 {fs::create_dir(root.join("releases").join(n.to_string())).unwrap();}
+        drop(Candidate::new(&root,1));
+        assert!(!root.join("releases/1").exists());
+        // A server may commit after this history read. No observed row is not a completed refusal.
+        let mut flight=Candidate::new(&root,2);flight.pending=true;drop(flight);
+        assert!(root.join("releases/2").is_dir());
+        store.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _release_events VALUES(3,NULL)",[]).unwrap();
+        drop(Candidate::new(&root,3));
+        assert!(root.join("releases/3").is_dir());
+    }
+}

new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..57faf2a03e
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/rollback_foresight.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,61 @@
+//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — future-write risks are distinct from snapshot validation.
+use snapback4_core::{Schema, successor::{self, Change}, schema::ColumnDefault};
+use std::path::Path;
+
+pub(super) fn risks(candidate: &Schema, old: &Schema, release: u64, dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
+    let mut risks:Vec<_>=successor::classify(candidate,old).into_iter().filter_map(|step| {
+        let path=step.path.strip_prefix("tables.")?;
+        let (table,column)=path.split_once(".columns.")?;
+        let target=old.tables.get(table)?;
+        if target.engine_field(column) || old.maintain_for(table).is_some() || target.maintained.contains_key(column) {return None;}
+        let before=target.columns.get(column)?;
+        let from=candidate.tables.get(table)?;
+        let reason=match from.columns.get(column) {
+            None if !before.is_optional() && !matches!(target.defaults.get(column),Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(_))) =>
+                "new rows will lack the old required field, so rollback can refuse".to_owned(),
+            Some(kind) if kind!=before && matches!(step.change,Change::Narrowing|Change::Destructive) =>
+                format!("new writes allowed by {kind} may not fit {before}, so rollback can refuse or hide rows"),
+            _ => return None,
+        };
+        Some(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, {table}.{column}: {reason}; keep an expand/contract bridge with the old required fields and compatible domains; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()))
+    }).collect();
+    for (name,target) in old.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
+        let Some(from)=candidate.tables.get(name) else {continue;};
+        if old.maintain_for(name).is_some() {continue;}
+        for (index,definition) in &target.indexes {
+            if definition.unique && from.indexes.get(index)!=Some(definition) {
+                risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, unique index {name}.{index}: future writes may introduce duplicate keys and refuse restoration; keep the old uniqueness in a bridge; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
+            }
+        }
+        if target.constraints.iter().any(|c|!from.constraints.contains(c) && matches!(c,snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{..}|snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Cap{..}|snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{..})) {
+            risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, table {name}: changed constraints may let future writes refuse rollback; preserve old bounds/quotas in a bridge; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
+        }
+    }
+    risks
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    fn schema(source:&str)->Schema {
+        let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
+        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b.schema
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn warns_before_any_future_rows_exist_but_compatible_additions_are_quiet() {
+        let base="table items:\n  required: int 0..10\n  state: enum('old')\n  public\n";
+        let old=schema(base);
+        for (candidate,subject) in [
+            (base.replace("  required: int 0..10\n",""),"items.required"),
+            (base.replace("int 0..10","int 0..20"),"items.required"),
+            (base.replace("enum('old')","enum('old','new')"),"items.state"),
+            (base.replace("int 0..10","int 0..10 ?"),"items.required"),
+        ] {
+            let warnings=risks(&schema(&candidate),&old,1,Path::new("deployment"));
+            assert!(warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains(subject)&&w.contains("--to 1 --check --bridge")),"{warnings:?}");
+        }
+        assert!(risks(&schema(&base.replace("  public","  label: text <=20 ?\n  public")),&old,1,Path::new("deployment")).is_empty());
+        let unique=schema(&base.replace("  public","  unique state\n  public"));
+        assert!(risks(&old,&unique,1,Path::new("deployment")).iter().any(|w|w.contains("duplicate keys")));
+    }
+}

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md
## 16. Release reactivation and rollback foresight (REL5)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-19; target 0.4.34.
This supersedes §10's reserved refused candidates and matching-latest refusal.
Compare captured executable bundle digests against successfully adopted releases,
including the active release. Equal active bytes are unchanged; equal retained
bytes select that release id, with rollback's restoration and safety checks.
No synthetic source edit or new release id is needed after rollback.
`--restore-dormant` is the common explicit restoration flag on deploy and
rollback; selecting a retained release already expresses restoration intent.
Fresh source still needs the flag when it reuses authored dormant names.
Compiler-owned `$intent:` index names do not express authored reuse intent;
their definitions/constraints still rebuild and validate normally. No restoration
flag bypasses types, constraints, references, audience or lifetime checks.

A new candidate is staged privately and validated before numeric publication.
The source must be durable before the database can select it. An activation
refusal removes its numeric candidate after database history proves it never
committed; a committed activation or an indeterminate/lost reply preserves source
for recovery. Submission tracking distinguishes no send, definitive reply and
transport ambiguity; a negative history read never licenses deletion while an
admitted request can still commit. History remains authoritative after post-commit pointer failures.
Preflight snapshots do not freeze later writes; live adoption still rechecks.
Ordinary refusals leave neither numbered candidates nor private stages.

Deploy and deploy --check report reverse compatibility against the currently
active release. Exact reverse adoption on the candidate's post-adoption private
snapshot reports present-data refusal. Static domain comparison also names
required old columns absent or optional in the candidate and candidate value
domains not contained in old domains: later writes can make rollback refuse or
hide rows. These are conservative risks, not claims every write causes failure.
The warning names old release/table/column and the command
`rollback <dir> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>`; keep an expand/contract bridge
with old required fields/defaults and compatible domains until repair is complete.
No warning changes admission or claims business safety.

Acceptance: cold and hot v1/v2/rollback/redeploy reuse v2's id and values;
uniqueness/missing-field restoration refusals preserve state and remove only
uncommitted candidates; no-op and check are read-only; post-commit source survives
ambiguous replies; dropped required columns and wider enum/int/text domains warn
even on empty stores, compatible additions stay quiet. Packed and three copied
day-two lane flows exercise the ordinary commands without workaround source edits.

llp/3000.000.000.000-release-spanning-clients-and-development-contracts.rfc.md
## 11. Old-client operation contracts (REL5)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-19; target 0.4.34. This
amends §3: new required parameters remain required in generated types, including
with a compatibility baseline. Added result fields retain release-spanning
fallbacks. check/dev with client-compatibility.json report old-client hazards
against that saved source; deploy/check and rollback/check compare the active
release's operation contracts to the candidate. Report removed or changed-kind
operations, added required arguments, removed arguments (unknown inputs refuse),
retyped/narrowed arguments, changed result shapes/domains and changed authored
refusals. A conservative operation footprint retains consumed source column domains
(including projected int/text bounds across writes, loops, merges and aggregates).
Guard/order dependencies and ambiguous same-name source fields may also appear
in RESULT REVIEW; this is an advisory to verify results/refusals, not a claim
that a returned value necessarily differs. A column never consumed by the
operation does not change this evidence. Declared action result domains compare
their full wire contracts, independently of TypeScript number/string erasure.
Each report names operation and source release/baseline, distinguishes
input breaks from result/behavior review, and gives an actionable repair. Correct
authorization tightening remains intentional behavior to verify, not an input
break. Static comparison is conservative and does not certify arbitrary behavior.

Quarry query/mutation parameters admit `name: T default <literal>`: omission
or null uses the fitting non-null literal, explicit non-null values validate
against T. Only bounded scalar literals (including negative integers) are
admitted; now/viewer/expressions and null defaults refuse. The compiler lowers
to an optional wire argument and ordinary coalescing setup in the shared IR;
the body sees T, generated callers may omit it. This adds no wire vocabulary.
`name: T deprecated` accepts a validated optional input but does not bind it
in the operation body; using it refuses compilation. This lets old callers send
an obsolete field while new callers omit it. It does not silently accept unknown
inputs. Modifiers on helper/shape/action/job/native declarations refuse explicitly
until their distinct invocation contracts support them.

`alias oldName = currentName` declares a query/mutation alias in the same
module. It clones the complete operation declaration before ordinary binding,
so validation, rules, guards, results, prediction classification and metering
remain the target's. Aliases target authored non-alias operations, cannot chain
or cycle, and collisions/unknown targets refuse. No alias can bypass authority.
Keep the alias until the old client population has retired.

Availability accepts extra optional served parameters; existing generated
parameter types must still match and no extra served required parameter may
exist. SDK and self-contained generated predicates share this contract. It
remains capability evidence, never authorization or result-semantic proof.

Acceptance covers omitted/null/explicit/invalid defaults through server and
device, ignored deprecated arguments and forbidden references, aliases including
module collisions and refusal parity, generated requiredness and availability
parity, argument/result/refusal diff controls, baseline warnings, and a packed
`guide deploy` v2 recipe whose frozen v1 calls still run with no client edits.

309: fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
310:     // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
311:     // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
312:     // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
313:     let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
314:     match result {
315:         Ok(()) => 0,
316:         Err(error) => {
317:             eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
318:             1
319:         }
320:     }
321: }
322: 
323: pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
324:     report(
325:         "deploy",
326:         (|| {
327:             let dir = canonical_directory(options)?;
328:             let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
329:             let existed = dir.join("store.sqlite").exists();
330:             let checking=options.flags.contains_key("check");
331:             let _deployment = if checking {None} else {target(&dir)?};
332:             if let Some(owner) = &_deployment { owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
333:             let project = super::proof_source::project(options, "schema")?;
334:             let sources = super::proof_source::sources(&project, options, "schema")?;

1: //! Operator preflight runs the real activation on a private disk-backed snapshot.
2: // REL3 owns dormant-visibility counts here; SURF clause narration lives in release_narration.
3: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#92-exact-preview-and-operator-vocabulary — no live pointer, generation or audit changes.
4: use super::*;
5: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
6: struct Snapshot(PathBuf);
7: impl Drop for Snapshot {
8:     fn drop(&mut self) {
9:         let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
10:     }
11: }
12: 
13: pub(super) fn preview(
14:     store_path: &Path,
15:     root: PathBuf,
16:     release: u64,
17:     options: &Options,
18: ) -> Result<(), String> {
19:     let bound = super::releases::bound_at(root.clone())?;
20:     let project = Project { root };
21:     let native = crate::native::prepare(&project, &bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
22:     crate::dev_worker::prepare_release(&project,&bound.schema)?;
23:     let id = snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default())
24:         .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
25:     let snapshot = Snapshot(std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("snapback4-release-preview-{id}")));
26:     snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&snapshot.0).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
27:     let path = snapshot.0.join("store.sqlite");
28:     // SQLite VACUUM INTO takes a coherent read snapshot even with a live WAL writer.
29:     // Busy/disk errors refuse this preview; never fall back to copying database bytes.
30:     if store_path.exists() {
31:     let source = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(store_path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
32:     source
33:         .conn()
34:         .execute("VACUUM INTO ?1", [path.to_string_lossy().as_ref()])
35:         .map_err(|e| {
36:             format!("snapshot preview failed (retry after store/disk becomes available): {e}")
37:         })?;
38:     drop(source);
39:     }
40:     let mut store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
41:     let held = store
42:         .release_status(0, None, None)
43:         .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
44:     let mut activation =
45:         snapback4_core::engine::release::ReleaseActivation::new(release, held["release"].as_u64());
46:     activation.expected_revision = held["revision"].as_u64();
47:     activation.native_digest = native.installed.map(|n| n.digest);
48:     activation.operation = "preview".into();
49:     activation.restore_dormant = !options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") || options.flags.contains_key("restore-dormant");
50:     let mut steps = Vec::new();
51:     let estimate = store
52:         .adoption_estimate(&bound.schema)
53:         .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
54:         .map(|e| e.summary());
55:     let mut client_compatibility=Vec::new();
56:     if let Some(current)=held["release"].as_u64() {
57:         let prior=super::releases::bound_at(store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?.join("releases").join(current.to_string()))?;
58:         client_compatibility=snapback4_lang::client_contracts::compare(&prior,&bound,&format!("release {current}"));
59:     }
60:     let candidate_schema=bound.schema.clone();
61:     let candidate_programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>=bound.programs.iter().map(|p|(p.program.name.clone(),p.program.clone())).collect();
62:     let mut hazards=Vec::new();
63:     let mut notes=Vec::new();
64:     let prior:Option<(String,String)>=store.conn().query_row("SELECT schema,programs FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?))).ok();
65:     let prior_schema=prior.as_ref().map(|(schema,_)|serde_json::from_str::<snapback4_core::Schema>(schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())).transpose()?;
66:     if let Some((schema,programs))=prior {
67:         let prior:snapback4_core::Schema=serde_json::from_str(&schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
68:         steps=snapback4_core::successor::classify(&prior,&candidate_schema);
69:         let programs:std::collections::BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::Program>=serde_json::from_str(&programs).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
70:         let target_label = format!("release {release}");
71:         let compatibility = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &prior, &candidate_schema, held["release"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0), &target_label, options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
72:         for line in compatibility {
73:             if let Some(warning) = line.strip_prefix("WARNING ") { hazards.push(warning.to_owned()); }
74:             else { notes.push(line.trim_start_matches("note: ").to_owned()); }
75:         }
76:         let rollback=!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction");
77:         let (behavior_warnings, behavior_notes) = super::release_narration::behavior(store_path, &store, &prior, &candidate_schema, &programs, &candidate_programs, &project, held["release"].as_u64(), &target_label, rollback)?;
78:         hazards.extend(behavior_warnings); notes.extend(behavior_notes);
79:         for name in prior.tables.keys().filter(|name|!candidate_schema.tables.contains_key(*name) && prior.maintain_for(name).is_none() && prior.tables[*name].ephemeral.is_none()) {
80:             let count:u64=store.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1)",[name],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
81:             if count>0 {hazards.push(format!("table {name}: {count} rows become dormant; {target_label} lacks this table's behavior; preserve it in a bridge or accept its absence"));}
82:         }
83:     }
84:     super::release_safeguards::capture(&store)?;
85:     let mut engine = snapback4_core::Engine::new_release_prepared(
86:         store,
87:         bound.schema,
88:         bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
89:         &activation,
90:         options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),
91:     )
92:     .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
93:     let mut planned = engine
94:         .store_mut()
95:         .release_status(0, None, None)
96:         .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
97:     planned["items"] = serde_json::to_value(
98:         engine
99:             .store_mut()
100:             .dormant_inventory(None, usize::MAX)
101:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
102:     )
103:     .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
104:     let items: Vec<snapback4_core::store::DormantItem> = serde_json::from_value(planned["items"].clone()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
105:     let mut effects = super::release_safeguards::warnings(engine.store(),prior_schema.as_ref(),&candidate_schema,&candidate_programs,&items,release,!options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction"))?;
106:     effects.append(&mut hazards); hazards=effects;
107:     for fill in &engine.store().adoption_report.backfills { notes.push(format!("backfill: {fill}; confirm these historical values before selecting release {release}")); }
108:     steps.retain(|step|!step.message.contains("engine capture"));
109:     for step in &mut steps {
110:         if step.change==snapback4_core::successor::Change::Narrowing {
111:             step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
112:             step.message=format!("{}; active rows satisfy the candidate after dormant projection",step.message.split(';').next().unwrap_or(&step.message));
113:         }
114:         if let Some(position)=step.path.strip_prefix("maintains[").and_then(|s|s.strip_suffix(']')).and_then(|s|s.parse::<usize>().ok()) {
115:             let label=candidate_schema.maintains[position].label();
116:             let visits=engine.store().adoption_report.views.iter().find(|view|view.name==label).map(|v|v.rows).unwrap_or(0);
117:             step.message=format!("maintain {label}: rebuilt from {visits} source rows");
118:             step.change=snapback4_core::successor::Change::Additive;
119:         }
120:         let path=step.path.strip_prefix("tables.").unwrap_or(&step.path).replace(".columns.",".");
121:         let count=planned["items"].as_array().into_iter().flatten().find(|i|i["path"].as_str()==Some(path.as_str())).and_then(|i|i["rows"].as_u64()).unwrap_or(0);
122:         let unit = if step.path.contains(".columns.") { "cells" } else if step.path.contains(".indexes.") { "entries" } else { "rows" };
123:         // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — per-path prose uses the same disjoint counts as the inventory totals.
124:         let retained = if count == 0 && unit == "cells" {
125:             format!("no standalone {unit} become dormant (cells of any dormant rows are counted with those rows)")
126:         } else if count == 0 {
127:             format!("no {unit} become dormant")
128:         } else {
129:             format!("its {unit} become dormant ({count} {unit}; restored on forward)")
130:         };
131:         step.message=step.message.replace("its rows are lost", &retained).replace("its values are lost", &retained);
132:     }
133:     if options.flags.contains_key("deploy-direction") {
134:         if let Some(current) = held["release"].as_u64() {
135:             let old_root=store_path.parent().ok_or("no deployment directory")?.join("releases").join(current.to_string());
136:             let old=super::releases::bound_at(old_root.clone())?;
137:             hazards.extend(super::rollback_foresight::risks(&candidate_schema,&old.schema,current,store_path.parent().unwrap()));
138:             // Exact reverse adoption tests today's post-deploy image, separately from future-write risks.
139:             let reverse_path=snapshot.0.join("reverse.sqlite");
140:             engine.store().conn().execute("VACUUM INTO ?1",[reverse_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref()]).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/release_files.rs
1: //! Immutable release custody: digest the required tree and flush it before DB selection.
2: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — source durability precedes the database's release pointer.
3: use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
4: use std::{
5:     fs,
6:     io::{Read, Write},
7:     path::{Path, PathBuf},
8: };
9: 
10: fn files(root: &Path, path: &Path, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) -> Result<(), String> {
11:     let mut entries = fs::read_dir(path)
12:         .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
13:         .map(|e| e.map(|e| e.path()).map_err(|e| e.to_string()))
14:         .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
15:     entries.sort();
16:     for path in entries {
17:         let relative = path.strip_prefix(root).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
18:         if relative
19:             .components()
20:             .next()
21:             .is_some_and(|c| c.as_os_str() == ".snapback4")
22:         {
23:             continue;
24:         }
25:         let m = fs::symlink_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
26:         if m.is_dir() {
27:             files(root, &path, out)?;
28:         } else if m.is_file() {
29:             out.push(path);
30:         } else {
31:             return Err(format!(
32:                 "immutable release artifact must be a real directory or regular file: {}",
33:                 path.display()
34:             ));
35:         }
36:     }
37:     Ok(())
38: }
39: pub(crate) fn digest(root: &Path) -> Result<String, String> {
40:     let mut paths = Vec::new();
41:     files(root, root, &mut paths)?;
42:     let mut hash = Sha256::new();
43:     for path in paths {
44:         let name = path.strip_prefix(root).unwrap().to_string_lossy();
45:         // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#4-cli-surface-contracts — metadata does not change executable release identity.
46:         if name == ".pending-adoption" || name == "provenance.json" {continue;}
47:         hash.update((name.len() as u64).to_le_bytes());
48:         hash.update(name.as_bytes());
49:         #[cfg(unix)]
50:         use std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
51:         let mut options = fs::OpenOptions::new();
52:         options.read(true);
53:         #[cfg(unix)]
54:         options.custom_flags(libc::O_NOFOLLOW);
55:         let mut file = options.open(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
56:         hash.update(
57:             file.metadata()
58:                 .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
59:                 .len()
60:                 .to_le_bytes(),
61:         );
62:         let mut buffer = [0u8; 16384];
63:         loop {
64:             let n = file.read(&mut buffer).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
65:             if n == 0 {
66:                 break;
67:             }
68:             hash.update(&buffer[..n]);
69:         }
70:     }
71:     Ok(format!("{:x}", hash.finalize()))
72: }
73: pub(crate) fn durable(root: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
74:     let mut paths = Vec::new();
75:     files(root, root, &mut paths)?;
76:     for path in paths {
77:         fs::File::open(path)
78:             .and_then(|f| f.sync_all())
79:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
80:     }
81:     fn dirs(path: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
82:         for e in fs::read_dir(path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
83:             let e = e.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
84:             if e.file_type().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_dir() {
85:                 dirs(&e.path())?;
86:             }
87:         }
88:         fs::File::open(path)
89:             .and_then(|f| f.sync_all())
90:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
91:     }
92:     dirs(root)

95: pub(crate) fn link_store(root: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
96:     let project = crate::project::Project { root: root.into() };
97:     snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&project.data_dir()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
98:     let path = project.store_path();
99:     #[cfg(unix)]
100:     {
101:         let target = Path::new("../../../store.sqlite");
102:         match fs::symlink_metadata(&path) {
103:             Ok(m) if m.file_type().is_symlink() => {
104:                 if fs::read_link(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())? != target {
105:                     fs::remove_file(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
106:                     std::os::unix::fs::symlink(target, &path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
107:                 }
108:             }
109:             Ok(_) => {
110:                 return Err(format!(
111:                     "release store path must be a symbolic link: {}",
112:                     path.display()
113:                 ));
114:             }
115:             Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
116:                 std::os::unix::fs::symlink(target, &path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
117:             }
118:             Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
119:         }
120:         Ok(())
121:     }
122:     #[cfg(not(unix))]
123:     Err("serving shared releases requires Unix symbolic links".into())
124: }

126: pub(crate) struct Pointer {
127:     staged: PathBuf,
128:     dir: PathBuf,
129:     release: u64,
130:     previous: Option<u64>,
131:     rollback: bool,
132:     ledger: fs::File,
133: }
134: impl Pointer {
135:     pub(crate) fn prepare(
136:         dir: &Path,
137:         release: u64,
138:         previous: Option<u64>,
139:         rollback: bool,
140:     ) -> Result<Self, String> {
141:         let pointer = dir.join("current");
142:         if pointer.exists() && !pointer.is_file() {
143:             return Err("current must be a regular release pointer file".into());
144:         }
145:         let staged = dir.join(format!(".current-{}-{release}", std::process::id()));
146:         let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::file(&staged, true).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
147:         let mut prepared = Self {
148:             staged,
149:             dir: dir.into(),
150:             release,
151:             previous,
152:             rollback,
153:             ledger: file,
154:         };
155:         writeln!(prepared.ledger, "{release}")
156:             .and_then(|_| prepared.ledger.sync_all())
157:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
158:         prepared.ledger = fs::OpenOptions::new()
159:             .create(true)
160:             .append(true)
161:             .open(dir.join("releases.jsonl"))
162:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
163:         writeln!(prepared.ledger,"{}",serde_json::json!({"at":snapback4_core::now_ms(),"release":release,"previous":previous,"rollback":rollback,"state":"prepared"})).and_then(|_|prepared.ledger.sync_all()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
164:         Ok(prepared)
165:     }
166:     pub(crate) fn finish(&mut self, generation: u64) -> Result<(), String> {
167:         fs::rename(&self.staged,self.dir.join("current")).and_then(|_|fs::File::open(&self.dir)?.sync_all()).map_err(|e|format!("release {} committed in database, but current copy failed: {e}; inspect `snapback4 releases {}`",self.release,self.dir.display()))?;
168:         writeln!(self.ledger,"{}",serde_json::json!({"at":snapback4_core::now_ms(),"release":self.release,"previous":self.previous,"rollback":self.rollback,"generation":generation,"state":"activated"})).and_then(|_|self.ledger.sync_all()).map_err(|e|format!("release committed, but ledger completion failed: {e}"))
169:     }
170: }
171: impl Drop for Pointer {
172:     fn drop(&mut self) {
173:         if self.staged.exists() {
174:             let _ = fs::remove_file(&self.staged);
175:         }
176:     }
177: }

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/operation_evolution.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/operation_evolution.rs
new file mode 100644

+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/operation_evolution.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,45 @@
+//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — operation evolution lowers into the existing shared IR.
+use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, type_error, unknown}};
+use snapback4_core::{Schema, ir::ArgType, value::Value};
+
+fn modified(args: &[ArgDecl]) -> bool { args.iter().any(|a|a.default.is_some() || a.deprecated) }
+fn invalid(site: &crate::Site) -> Diagnostic {
+    type_error(site,"argument defaults/deprecation are supported on non-native queries and mutations only","keep ordinary parameters here; use a query/mutation for a release-compatible wrapper").guide("deploy")
+}
+pub(super) fn expand(documents: &mut [Document], diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
+    for doc in documents {
+        for p in &doc.programs {
+            if (p.native || p.kind==ProgramKind::Job) && modified(&p.args) {diagnostics.push(invalid(&p.site));}
+        }
+        for a in &doc.actions {if modified(&a.args) {diagnostics.push(invalid(&a.site));}}
+        for f in &doc.functions {if modified(&f.args) {diagnostics.push(invalid(&f.site));}}
+        let authored=doc.programs.clone();
+        for (name,target,site) in &doc.aliases {
+            let Some(p)=authored.iter().find(|p|p.name==*target && !p.native && p.kind!=ProgramKind::Job) else {
+                diagnostics.push(unknown(site,format!("alias {name} needs an authored query/mutation target {target} in this module"),"target a non-native query or mutation; aliases cannot chain").guide("deploy"));continue;
+            };
+            let mut alias=p.clone();alias.name=name.clone();alias.site=site.clone();doc.programs.push(alias);
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+pub(super) fn argument(arg: &ArgDecl, schema: &Schema) -> Fallible<(ArgType,Option<Value>)> {
+    let kind=crate::types::scalar_to_arg(&arg.type_);
+    let bad=||type_error(&arg.site,format!("default for {} must be a fitting non-null scalar literal and cannot combine with deprecated",arg.name),"use default <literal>, or mark the unused parameter deprecated").guide("deploy");
+    let value=if let Some(expr)=&arg.default {
+        if arg.deprecated {return Err(bad());}
+        let value=match expr {
+            Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Bool(v),..}=>Value::Bool(*v),
+            Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(v),..}=>match arg.type_.kind {TypeKind::Ref{..}|TypeKind::Id|TypeKind::Principal=>Value::Id(v.clone()),_=>Value::String(v.clone())},
+            Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Number(v),..}=>Value::Int(v.parse().map_err(|_|bad())?),
+            Expr::Unary {op,value,..} if op=="-" => match value.as_ref() {Expr::Literal{value:Literal::Number(v),..}=>Value::Int(format!("-{v}").parse().map_err(|_|bad())?),_=>return Err(bad())},
+            _=>return Err(bad()),
+        };
+        snapback4_core::engine::validate::validate_arg(schema,&kind,&value,&arg.name).map_err(|_|bad())?;
+        Some(value)
+    } else {None};
+    let wire=if arg.default.is_some() || arg.deprecated {
+        match kind {ArgType::Optional(_)=>kind,_=>ArgType::Optional(Box::new(kind))}
+    } else {kind};
+    Ok((wire,value))
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/client_contracts.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/client_contracts.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..f0e3a2b295
--- /dev/null

@@ -0,0 +1,92 @@
+//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — compare old callers with the candidate, separately from data adoption.
+use crate::{Bound, BoundProgram, types::Ty};
+use snapback4_core::ir::{ArgType as A, ProgramKind};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+
+/// Conservative input-domain inclusion: every value accepted by old is accepted by new.
+pub fn accepts(old: &A, new: &A) -> bool {
+    if old==new {return true;}
+    match (old,new) {
+        (A::Optional(a),A::Optional(b))=>accepts(a,b),
+        (A::Optional(_),_)=>false,
+        (a,A::Optional(b))=>accepts(a,b),
+        (A::Text{min:a,max:b,format:af},A::Text{min:c,max:d,format:bf})=>c<=a && d>=b && (bf.is_none() || af==bf),
+        (A::IntRange{..},A::Int)=>true,
+        (A::IntRange{min:a,max:b},A::IntRange{min:c,max:d})=>c<=a && d>=b,
+        (A::Enum{variants:a},A::Enum{variants:b})=>a.iter().all(|v|b.contains(v)),
+        (A::Bytes{max:a},A::Bytes{max:b}) | (A::Image{max:a},A::Image{max:b}) |
+        (A::Video{max:a},A::Video{max:b})=>b>=a,
+        (A::Json{max_bytes:a},A::Json{max_bytes:b})=>b>=a,
+        (A::File{max:a,types:at},A::File{max:b,types:bt})=>b>=a && snapback4_core::assets::FILE_TYPES.iter().all(|t|!snapback4_core::assets::file_accepts(at,t)||snapback4_core::assets::file_accepts(bt,t)),
+        (A::List{..}|A::ListRange{..},A::List{..}|A::ListRange{..})=> {
+            let parts=|t:&A|match t {A::List{of,max}=>(0,*max,of.clone()),A::ListRange{of,min,max}=>(*min,*max,of.clone()),_=>unreachable!()};
+            let (a,b,at)=parts(old);let(c,d,bt)=parts(new);
+            c<=a && d>=b && match(at,bt){(_,None)=>true,(Some(a),Some(b))=>accepts(&a,&b),_=>false}
+        }
+        _=>false,
+    }
+}
+
+fn result(ty: &Ty, bound: &Bound) -> String {
+    match ty {
+        Ty::Row(name)|Ty::WriteRow(name)|Ty::Page(name,_)=> {
+            let schema=bound.schema.public_projection();
+            if let Some(table)=schema.tables.get(name) {
+                let fields: BTreeMap<_,_>=table.columns.iter().filter(|(n,_)|!table.engine_field(n)).map(|(n,t)|(n,format!("{t:?};private={};constraints={:?}",table.private_field(n),table.constraints.iter().filter(|c|matches!(c,snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field,..} if field==n)).collect::<Vec<_>>()))).collect();
+                format!("{}:{name}:{fields:?}",if matches!(ty,Ty::Page(..)){"page"}else{"row"})
+            } else {format!("{ty:?}")}
+        }
+        Ty::Optional(t)=>format!("optional {}",result(t,bound)),
+        Ty::List(t,_)|Ty::Collection(t,_)=>format!("list {}",result(t,bound)),
+        Ty::Object(fields)|Ty::RowShape(fields)|Ty::RowObject(fields)=>format!("object {:?}",fields.iter().map(|(n,t)|(n,result(t,bound))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>()),
+        Ty::Text(max)=>format!("text<={max}"),
+        _=>format!("{ty:?}"),
+    }
+}
+
+fn refusals(p: &BoundProgram) -> Vec<String> {
+    fn walk(v: &serde_json::Value, out: &mut Vec<String>) {
+        match v {
+            serde_json::Value::Object(o)=> {
+                if let Some(r)=o.get("Require") {out.push(format!("{}:{}",r["code"],r["message"]));}
+                for v in o.values(){walk(v,out);}
+            }
+            serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{walk(v,out);},
+            _=>{}
+        }
+    }
+    let mut out=Vec::new();walk(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program.body).unwrap(),&mut out);out
+}
+
+/// Reports are advisory: compatibility cannot license calls or prove unchanged behavior.
+pub fn compare(old: &Bound, new: &Bound, label: &str) -> Vec<String> {
+    let mut lines=Vec::new();
+    for before in old.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind!=ProgramKind::Job) {
+        let name=&before.program.name;
+        let prefix=format!("old-client compatibility ({label}), operation {name}");
+        let Some(after)=new.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==*name) else {
+            lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: removed operation; old calls get E_OP; keep `alias {name} = replacement` for a same-module query/mutation, or retain a wrapper"));continue;
+        };
+        if before.program.kind!=after.program.kind {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: operation kind changed; retain the old endpoint kind"));continue;}
+        for (arg,kind) in &after.program.args {
+            if !before.program.args.contains_key(arg) && !matches!(kind,A::Optional(_)) {
+                lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: clients calling without new required argument {arg} get E_INPUT; give {arg} a fitting `default <literal>` or retain a compatible wrapper"));
+            }
+        }
+        for (arg,kind) in &before.program.args {
+            match after.program.args.get(arg) {
+                None=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: removed argument {arg}; old calls sending it get E_INPUT; retain `{arg}: <old type> deprecated` on a query/mutation")),
+                Some(next) if !accepts(kind,next)=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: argument {arg} retyped or narrowed ({kind:?} → {next:?}); old values may get E_INPUT; retain the old input domain or a compatible wrapper")),
+                _=>{}
+            }
+        }
+        let action_result_changed=old.schema.action_specs.get(name).map(|s|&s.returns)!=new.schema.action_specs.get(name).map(|s|&s.returns);
+        if action_result_changed || result(&before.result,old)!=result(&after.result,new) {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT REVIEW: result shape/value domain changed; preserve the old projection/domain or introduce a new operation before retiring old clients"));}
+        let old_domains=crate::result_domains::signature(before,old);
+        let new_domains=crate::result_domains::signature(after,new);
+        let changed:Vec<_>=old_domains.keys().chain(new_domains.keys()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().filter(|key|old_domains.get(*key)!=new_domains.get(*key)).cloned().collect();
+        if !changed.is_empty() {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT REVIEW: declared source domains consumed by this operation changed: {}; results or refusals may change even when the TypeScript type stays number/string; preserve the old domains or verify old-client handling",changed.join(", ")));}
+        if refusals(before)!=refusals(after) {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: BEHAVIOR REVIEW: refusal codes/messages changed ({:?} → {:?}); verify intended denials separately from input breaks",refusals(before),refusals(after)));}
+    }
+    lines
+}
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/availability.ts b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/availability.ts

@@ -7,6 +7,8 @@ function operationAvailable(release: ReleaseSnapshot, name: string, kind: string
   const canonical = (value: unknown): string => JSON.stringify(value, (_, item) => object(item)
     ? Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(item).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCompare(b))) : item);
   const op = release.operations[name];
-  return !!op && op.kind === kind && canonical(op.args) === canonical(args);
+  return !!op && op.kind === kind
+    && Object.entries(args).every(([name, type]) => canonical(op.args[name]) === canonical(type))
+    && Object.entries(op.args).every(([name, type]) => Object.hasOwn(args, name) || (object(type) && Object.hasOwn(type, "Optional")));
 }
 
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/compat.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/compat.rs

@@ -1,11 +1,11 @@
-//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#3-generated-compatibility — opt-in types span a saved baseline and current source.
+//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — opt-in types span a saved baseline and current source.
 use super::*;
 
 pub(super) fn args(current: &[(String, Ty)], old: Option<&[(String, Ty)]>, since: Option<u64>) -> String {
     if current.is_empty() { return "Record<string, never>".into(); }
     format!("{{ {} }}", current.iter().map(|(name, ty)| {

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/result_domains.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/result_domains.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..17e9ca7cd4
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/result_domains.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,49 @@
+//! Conservative source-domain evidence for operation results and refusal behavior.
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — TypeScript numbers/strings must not erase consumed source-domain changes.
+use crate::{Bound,BoundProgram};
+use serde_json::Value;
+use std::collections::{BTreeMap,BTreeSet};
+
+// Inspect the complete operation: result construction may flow through writes,
+// loops, merges, helper blocks or aggregate selectors. This deliberately includes
+// guard/order dependencies rather than claiming a partial lineage analysis is exact.
+fn consumed(value:&Value,tables:&mut BTreeSet<String>,fields:&mut BTreeSet<String>) {
+    match value {
+        Value::Object(object)=> {
+            // Read, scan, merge lane, recursion and write targets all carry table.
+            if let Some(table)=object.get("table").and_then(Value::as_str) {tables.insert(table.into());}
+            if let Some(field)=object.get("Field") && let Some(name)=field["name"].as_str() {fields.insert(name.into());}
+            // Aggregate, optional-field, range and sort selectors carry field directly.
+            if let Some(field)=object.get("field").and_then(Value::as_str) {fields.insert(field.into());}
+            for child in object.values() {consumed(child,tables,fields);}
+        }
+        Value::Array(array)=>for child in array {consumed(child,tables,fields);},
+        _=>{}
+    }
+}
+fn row_fields(ty:&crate::types::Ty,bound:&Bound,fields:&mut BTreeSet<String>) {
+    use crate::types::Ty;
+    match ty {
+        Ty::Row(name)|Ty::WriteRow(name)|Ty::Page(name,_)=>if let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(name) {fields.extend(table.columns.keys().cloned());},
+        Ty::RowShape(parts)|Ty::RowObject(parts)=>{fields.extend(parts.keys().cloned());for ty in parts.values(){row_fields(ty,bound,fields);}},
+        Ty::Object(parts)=>for ty in parts.values(){row_fields(ty,bound,fields);},
+        Ty::Optional(ty)|Ty::List(ty,_)|Ty::Collection(ty,_)=>row_fields(ty,bound,fields),
+        _=>{}
+    }
+}
+pub(crate) fn signature(program:&BoundProgram,bound:&Bound)->BTreeMap<String,String> {
+    let mut tables=BTreeSet::new();let mut fields=BTreeSet::new();
+    consumed(&serde_json::to_value(&program.program.body).unwrap(),&mut tables,&mut fields);
+    row_fields(&program.result,bound,&mut fields);
+    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
+    for name in tables {
+        let Some(table)=bound.schema.table(&name) else {continue;};
+        for field in &fields {
+            if let Some(kind)=table.columns.get(field) {
+                let constraints:Vec<_>=table.constraints.iter().filter(|c|matches!(c,snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field:name,..} if name==field)).collect();
+                out.insert(format!("{name}.{field}"),format!("{kind:?};{constraints:?}"));
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    out
+}
