1: //! Immutable source releases with a database-authoritative activation pointer.
2: use super::*;
3: use crate::store_owner::Owner;
4: use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
5: #[path = "staging.rs"]
6: mod staging;
7: struct Stage(PathBuf);
8: impl Drop for Stage {fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}
9: 
10: fn directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
11:     options
12:         .positionals
13:         .first()
14:         .map(PathBuf::from)
15:         .ok_or_else(|| "a deployment directory is required".into())
16: }
17: 
18: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5.1 — deployment aliases share ownership, control and worker paths.
19: fn canonical_directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
20:     let requested = directory(options)?;
21:     if !requested.exists() {
22:         snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&requested).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
23:     }
24:     let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
25:     snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&dir).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
26:     Ok(dir)
27: }
28: 
29: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#8 — validate before any writable ownership or command lock.
30: pub(crate) fn existing_deployment(requested: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
31:     let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", requested.display(), requested.display());
32:     if !requested.join("store.sqlite").is_file() { return Err(missing()); }
33:     let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
34:     inspect_current(&dir)?;
35:     Ok(dir)
36: }
37: 
38: // SQLite READONLY can create WAL sidecars. A stopped store's existing owner
39: // lock makes immutable inspection safe; a live host supplies its own status.
40: fn inspect_current(dir: &Path) -> Result<u64, String> {
41:     use rusqlite::{Connection, OpenFlags, OptionalExtension};
42:     let database = dir.join("store.sqlite").canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
43:     let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", dir.display(), dir.display());
44:     let owner = match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,
45:         &snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database), false, 0) {
46:         Ok(owner) => owner,
47:         Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
48:             // Relocated/legacy deployments may not yet have a sidecar beside
49:             // the resolved DB. Establish a complete current source first; only
50:             // a known deployment may let SQLite create its ordinary WAL files.
51:             return pointer_current(dir, &database);
52:         },
53:         Err(error) => return Err(format!("{}; existing ownership sidecar unavailable: {error}", missing())),
54:     };
55:     match owner.try_lock() {
56:         Ok(()) => {},
57:         Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => {
58:             match crate::release_control::request(dir, crate::release_control::Command::Status {after:0, items_after:None, event:None}) {
59:                 Ok(status) => return status["release"].as_u64().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing),
60:                 Err(error) => {
61:                     // A killed host's watchdogs retain ownership during their
62:                     // bounded cleanup. Preserve serve's existing restart wait.
63:                     if !crate::store_owner::stopped_host(&database) {
64:                         // A second source directory may alias the same DB. Its
65:                         // source cannot control this host, but its token still
66:                         // belongs to the resolved database. Establish current
67:                         // source before permitting ordinary read-only WAL work.
68:                         return pointer_current(dir, &database).map_err(|_| format!("{}; {error}",missing()));
69:                     }
70:                     let deadline=std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_secs(5);
71:                     loop {
72:                         match owner.try_lock() {
73:                             Ok(()) => break,
74:                             Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) if std::time::Instant::now()<deadline => std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(25)),
75:                             _ => return Err(format!("{}; {error}",missing())),
76:                         }
77:                     }
78:                 }
79:             }
80:         },
81:         Err(error) => return Err(error.to_string()),
82:     }
83:     if !snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_is_named(&database, &owner) {
84:         return Err("store ownership changed during release inspection; retry".into());
85:     }
86:     #[cfg(unix)]
87:     let bytes = { use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt; database.as_os_str().as_bytes() };
88:     #[cfg(not(unix))]
89:     let bytes = database.to_str().ok_or("invalid store path")?.as_bytes();
90:     let mut uri = String::from("file:");
91:     for byte in bytes {
92:         if byte.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"/._-".contains(byte) { uri.push(*byte as char); }
93:         else { uri.push_str(&format!("%{byte:02X}")); }
94:     }
95:     let mut wal = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); wal.push("-wal"); let wal = PathBuf::from(wal);
96:     let mut shm = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); shm.push("-shm"); let shm = PathBuf::from(shm);
97:     let has_wal = match std::fs::metadata(&wal) {
98:         Ok(meta) => meta.len() > 0,
99:         Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => false,
100:         Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
101:     };
102:     if has_wal && !shm.is_file() { return Err("store has a WAL without its shared-memory sidecar; run deploy on this stopped deployment to recover it before inspection".into()); }
103:     // Immutable SQLite omits WAL reads: use it only for the checkpointed main
104:     // image. Existing WAL+SHM can be read normally under the same exclusion.
105:     uri.push_str(if has_wal { "?mode=ro" } else { "?mode=ro&immutable=1" });
106:     let conn = Connection::open_with_flags(uri, OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY | OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_URI).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
107:     let release: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release'", [], |row|row.get(0)).optional().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
108:     let release = match release {
109:         Some(value) => value,
110:         None => std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")).map_err(|_| missing())?,
111:     };
112:     release.trim().parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing)
113: }
114: 
115: fn pointer_current(dir: &Path, database: &Path) -> Result<u64, String> {
116:     let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; deploy first", dir.display());
117:     let pointer = std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")).map_err(|_| missing())?;
118:     let pointer = pointer.trim().parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing)?;
119:     bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(pointer.to_string()))?;
120:     let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(database).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
121:     match store.meta("release").map_err(|e|e.to_string())? {
122:         Some(value) => value.parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing),
123:         None => Ok(pointer),
124:     }
125: }
126: 
127: /// `dev` and `serve` hold the store-owner lock for their lifetime. Release
128: /// commands route to the admitted server when it owns this deployment.
129: pub(crate) fn deployment_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> {
130:     crate::store_owner::command(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))
131: }
132: fn current(dir: &Path, owner: &Owner) -> Result<Option<u64>, String> {
133:     let database = dir.join("store.sqlite");
134:     if database.exists() {
135:         let store = owner
136:             .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
137:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
138:         if let Some(release) = store.meta("release").map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
139:             return release
140:                 .parse()
141:                 .map(Some)
142:                 .map_err(|_| "invalid release identity in store".into());
143:         }
144:     }
145:     // Compatibility with deployments made before the database owned this
146:     // pointer. The next activation records it transactionally.
147:     match std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")) {
148:         Ok(text) => text
149:             .trim()
150:             .parse()
151:             .map(Some)
152:             .map_err(|_| "invalid current release file".into()),
153:         Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
154:         Err(e) => Err(format!("cannot read current release: {e}")),
155:     }
156: }
157: pub(super) fn bound_at(root: PathBuf) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, String> {
158:     let project = Project { root };
159:     if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
160:         return Err("release has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release".into());
161:     }
162:     let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
163:     if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
164:         return Err(bound
165:             .diagnostics
166:             .iter()
167:             .map(ToString::to_string)
168:             .collect::<Vec<_>>()
169:             .join("\n"));
170:     }
171:     Ok(bound)
172: }
173: fn check_change(
174:     dir: &Path,
175:     previous: Option<u64>,
176:     bound: &snapback4_lang::Bound,
177:     options: &Options,
178:     target_release: Option<u64>,
179: ) -> Result<(), String> {
180:     if !options.flags.contains_key("check") && let Some(n) = previous {
181:         let old = bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string()))?;
182:         let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
183:         let target_label = target_release.map(|n| format!("release {n}")).unwrap_or_else(|| "the new release".into());
184:         let lines = super::release_narration::compatibility(&store, &old.schema, &bound.schema, n, &target_label, target_release.is_none())?;
185:         for line in lines {
186:             eprintln!("{line}");
187:         }
188:     }
189:     if options.flags.contains_key("allow-destructive") {
190:         eprintln!(
191:             "deploy/rollback retain storage; --allow-destructive does not bypass validation or prune. To drop dormant storage: snapback4 prune {} --allow-destructive",
192:             dir.display()
193:         );
194:     }
195:     Ok(())
196: }

214: fn target(dir: &Path) -> Result<Option<Owner>, String> {

218:         Err(error) => Err(error),
219:     }

12:     for name in ["current","original"] {
13:         for args in [BTreeMap::new(),BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Null)]),BTreeMap::from([("old".into(),Value::String("legacy".into()))])] {
14:             assert_eq!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",args)).unwrap().data,Value::Int(4));
15:         }
16:         assert_eq!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(6))]))).unwrap().data,Value::Int(6));
17:         assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(11))]))).is_err());
18:         assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("n".into(),Value::Int(9))]))).is_err());
19:         assert!(engine.query(name,Context::new("user:a",BTreeMap::from([("old".into(),Value::Int(2))]))).is_err());
20:     }
21: }
22: #[test]
23: fn invalid_modifiers_and_aliases_refuse_without_silent_semantic_changes() {
24:     for source in [
25:         "query q(n: int default 11):\n  return n\n".replace("int default", "int 0..10 default"),
26:         "query q(n: int default now):\n  return n\n".into(),
27:         "query q(n: int default null):\n  return n\n".into(),
28:         "query q(n: int default 1 deprecated):\n  return 1\n".into(),
29:         "query q(n: int deprecated):\n  return n\n".into(),
30:         "fn f(n: int default 1):\n  return n\nquery q():\n  return f(1)\n".into(),
31:         "job j(n: int default 1): native\n".into(),
32:         "query q():\n  return 1\nalias a = q\nalias b = a\n".into(),
33:         "query q():\n  return 1\nalias q = q\n".into(),
34:         "alias a = b\nalias b = a\n".into(),
35:     ] {assert!(!compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.clone())]).diagnostics.is_empty(),"{source}");}
36: }
37: #[test]
38: fn reports_old_input_breaks_results_and_refusals_and_keeps_required_types() {
39:     let old=checked("query removed():\n  return true\nquery q(n: int 0..10, obsolete: text <=20):\n  require n != 0 else ZERO\n  return {n}\n");
40:     let new=checked("query q(n: int 1..9, added: bool):\n  require n != 1 else ONE\n  return {value: n}\n");
41:     let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1").join("\n");
42:     for text in ["removed operation","new required argument added","removed argument obsolete","argument n retyped or narrowed","RESULT REVIEW","BEHAVIOR REVIEW","release 1"] {assert!(report.contains(text),"{text}: {report}");}
43:     let api=emit::typescript_spanning(&new,&old,2);
44:     assert!(api.contains("*/ added: boolean"),"{api}");assert!(!api.contains("added?:"));
45:     let safe=checked("query current(n: int 0..20, obsolete: text <=20 deprecated, added: bool default false):\n  require n != 0 else ZERO\n  return {n}\nalias q = current\nalias removed = constant\nquery constant():\n  return true\n");
46:     assert!(compare(&old,&safe,"release 1").is_empty(),"{:?}",compare(&old,&safe,"release 1"));
47: }
48: #[test]
49: fn same_named_nominal_rows_still_report_changed_result_fields() {
50:     let old=checked("table items:\n  n: int\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n");
51:     let new=checked("table items:\n  n: int\n  label: text <=20 default ''\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n");
52:     assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|s|s.contains("RESULT REVIEW")));
53: }
54: 
55: #[test]
56: fn defaults_and_aliases_have_device_parity_after_backend_json_roundtrip() {
57:     use snapback4_device::{device::{Backend,Device},memory::MemoryStore};
58:     let b=checked("query q(n: int default -4, old: bool deprecated):\n  return n\nalias legacy = q\n");
59:     let backend=Backend{generation:1,schema:b.schema,programs:b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program).collect()};
60:     let encoded=serde_json::to_string(&backend).unwrap();
61:     let decoded:Backend=snapback4_core::json_codec::from_text(&encoded).unwrap();
62:     let mut device=Device::open_store(MemoryStore::new(),Some(decoded)).unwrap();
63:     for op in ["q","legacy"] {
64:         for args in [serde_json::json!({}),serde_json::json!({"n":null,"old":true})] {
65:             assert_eq!(device.query(op,"user:a",&args,0).unwrap().data,Value::Int(-4));
66:         }
67:         assert_eq!(device.query(op,"user:a",&serde_json::json!({"n":7}),0).unwrap().data,Value::Int(7));
68:         assert!(device.query(op,"user:a",&serde_json::json!({"n":"bad"}),0).is_err());
69:     }
70: }
71: 
72: #[test]
73: fn projected_results_keep_declared_integer_and_text_domains() {
74:     let source="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  public\nquery q(id: items):\n  row = items[id]! else NOT_FOUND\n  return {n: row.n, label: row.label}\n";
75:     let old=checked(source);
76:     for (from,to,field) in [("int 0..10","int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..40","items.label")] {
77:         let new=checked(&source.replacen(from,to,1));
78:         assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{:?}",compare(&old,&new,"release 1"));
79:     }
80:     let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
81:     assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{:?}",compare(&old,&unused,"release 1"));
82: }
83: 
84: #[test]
85: fn projected_pages_and_accumulated_lists_keep_declared_result_domains() {
86:     let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  public\n";
87:     for body in ["query q():\n  return items first 10 by .id {n, label}\n", "query q():\n  out = []\n  for row in items first 10 by .id:\n    out = out + [{n: row.n, label: row.label}]\n  return out\n"] {
88:         let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);
89:         for (from,to,field) in [("n: int 0..10","n: int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..20","items.label")] {
90:             let new=checked(&source.replace(from,to));let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1");
91:             assert!(report.iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{body}: {report:?}");
92:         }
93:         let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
94:         assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{body}");
95:     }
96: }

98: #[test]
99: fn writes_walks_and_aggregate_selectors_keep_result_domains() {
100:     let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  unused: int 0..10\n  parent: items ?\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n";
101:     for body in [
102:         "mutation q(id: items):\n  row = update items[id] {label: 'x'}\n  return {n: row.n, label: row.label}\n",
103:         "query q(id: items):\n  chain = walk items[id] up .parent depth 8\n  return chain {n, label}\n",
104:         "query q():\n  page = items first 10 by .id\n  return {n: min(page, .n), label: min(page, .label)}\n",
105:     ] {
106:         let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);
107:         for (from,to,field) in [("n: int 0..10","n: int 0..20","items.n"),("text 1..20","text 0..20","items.label")] {
108:             let new=checked(&source.replace(from,to));let report=compare(&old,&new,"release 1");
109:             assert!(report.iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")&&line.contains(field)),"{body}: {report:?}");
110:         }
111:         let unused=checked(&source.replace("unused: int 0..10","unused: int 0..100"));
112:         assert!(compare(&old,&unused,"release 1").is_empty(),"{body}: {:?}",compare(&old,&unused,"release 1"));
113:     }
114: }
115: #[test]
116: fn declared_action_results_keep_bounds_that_typescript_erases() {

125: fn whole_rows_and_write_images_keep_declared_text_minima() {
126:     let table="table items:\n  n: int 0..10\n  label: text 1..20\n  public\n  update <- allow\n";
127:     for body in ["query q(id: items):\n  return items[id]\n", "query q():\n  return items first 10 by .id\n", "mutation q(id: items):\n  return update items[id] {n: 1}\n"] {
128:         let source=format!("{table}{body}");let old=checked(&source);let new=checked(&source.replace("text 1..20","text 0..20"));
129:         assert!(compare(&old,&new,"release 1").iter().any(|line|line.contains("RESULT REVIEW")),"{body}");
130:     }
131: }

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md
1: # Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution
2: 
3: A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:
4: 
5: ```sh
6: bunx snapback4 check
7: bunx snapback4 test
8: bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
9: bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
10: ```
11: For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`).
12: 
13: ## Upgrade
14: `snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.
15: 
16: ## Deploy and password identity
17: Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
18: Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
19: ```sh
20: U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
21: curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
22:   -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
23: # {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
24: curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
25:   -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
26: ```
27: Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness.
28: 
29: Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.
30: 
31: ## First admin: a service seat
32: Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
33: ```quarry
34: use identity password
35: table admins:
36:   principal: principal
37:   unique principal
38:   immutable principal
39:   sync
40:   read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
41:   insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
42: mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
43:   return insert admins { principal }
44: table notes:
45:   owner: principal
46:   body: text <=200
47:   archived: bool default false
48:   status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
49:   read <- .owner = viewer
50:   insert <- .owner = viewer
51: ```
52: ```sh
53: TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
54: curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
55:   -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
56: unset TOKEN
57: ```
58: This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads.
59: 
60: A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.
61: 
62: ## Migrations and adoption
63: Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or explicit backfill; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.
64: 
65: ## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
66: ```sh
67: bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
68: bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
69: ```
70: `deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused deploy removes its candidate directory and private stage. An ambiguous reply preserves source until committed history resolves the outcome; inspect releases before retrying. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Source identical to the active release is a no-op; source matching a retained release reactivates its existing id with restoration and full validation. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.
71: 
72: Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; selecting retained source by deploy or rollback already selects restoration. The same --restore-dormant flag is accepted by both commands. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).
73: 
74: Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.
75: 
76: ## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
77: Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.
78: 
79: 1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
80: 2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
81: 3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
82: 4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.
83: 
84: ## What the old release sees
85: | Request after rollback | Result |
86: |---|---|
87: | An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
88: | Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
89: | Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
90: | Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
91: | Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
92: | Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |
93: 
94: This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
95: ```js
96: await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
97: assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
98: assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
99: assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
100: await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
101: ```
102: Previews lead with removed guards and their refusal codes, then group query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows and safety dependencies per change. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.
103: 
104: ## Clients across rollback
105: Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. Required operation parameters remain required; only declared optional, defaulted or deprecated arguments may be omitted. Baseline generation also warns about old-client input breaks and result/refusal changes. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.
106: 
107: `useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/existing-argument signatures differ or the server adds a required argument; extra optional served parameters are compatible. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.
108: 
109: ## Trigger a deployed job
110: A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
111: ```sh
112: export SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
113: bunx snapback4 run reconcile --release ./deployment --json
114: unset SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN
115: ```
116: `--token-env NAME` reads another environment variable. The command uses served source, so it also works outside the app directory. A lost reply is ambiguous; inspect the queue before retrying. Starter/client recipes: `guide client`.
117: 
118: ## Persona transition checklist
119: The complete account directory, linked membership and real-account transition is `guide production`.
120: 1. Add password identity and signup/login UI; persist sessions with localStorageSessionStore on web or the secure native store, and verify restart.
121: 2. Replace hard-coded persona owners/UI with signed-in account principals; map roles through the trusted seat recipe.
122: 3. Give profiles a production write path and authorized directory; expose no credentials.
123: 4. Create required teams/memberships, invitations or principal sharing; wait for scope delivery before offline reads; test expiry, revocation and outsiders.
124: 5. Bootstrap every required table without fixtures/seed; guide start/client covers first writers and password starters.
125: 6. Prove real-account writes, role checks, sign-out and rollback on durable storage behind TLS.
126: 
127: ## Inspection, proxies and TLS
128: | Door | `dev` | `serve` |
129: |---|---|---|
130: | `/q`, `/m`, `GET /` compatibility record | Loopback persona or bearer | Bearer session required, including public rows |
131: | `/auth/config`; signup/login/guest | Public config; declared sign-in methods | Public config; declared sign-in methods |
132: | `/seed`, `/dev/*` | Development only | Refused |
133: | `/jobs/<job>` | Service seat | Full service seat |
134: 
135: Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).
136: 
137: `snapback4 sql 'SELECT id, archived FROM notes' --release ./deployment --json` returns positional rows under `columns`; `--json-objects` returns object rows with direct boolean columns as true/false. Schema-qualified relations, recursive or shadowing/forward-reference CTEs need positional --json. Inspection bypasses read rules and can show expired stored rows; it is an operator tool, not an app query surface. Bind loopback behind a TLS proxy. Forward Authorization and preserve response bodies; allow long polls without buffering or early timeouts. Non-loopback HTTP requires `--i-understand-cleartext` and a trusted private hop behind TLS. Identity rate keys use the socket peer, not forwarded headers. Serve refuses personas and dev doors even through a local proxy; see SERVER.md for capacity. `--json-objects` returns `{header, columns, rows, limit, more, ruleFiltered:false}` with object `rows`; `--json` uses positional `rows`. Put flags outside the quoted SQL.
138: 
139: ## Authoring and proof refusals
140: N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER means no production insert path can bootstrap this table. It groups root blockers with their dependents; persona-only schemas get one identity note. Add a service-seat insert mutation and provision real account relationships. E_UPGRADE means installation or post-upgrade check failed. Repair the package-manager or registry error and rerun upgrade. Packages may already be pinned: if check failed, repair the reported schema/device refusal and rerun check.
141: 
142: ## Capture a saved version
143: After check and business cases pass, from the app root:
144: ```sh
145: snapback4 deploy ./deployment --save-version versions/v1
146: snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check --bridge ./bridge
147: snapback4 deploy ./deployment --schema ./bridge --check
148: ```
149: 
150: The new versions/v1 captures selected modules, their contemporary fixtures and `versions/v1/release.json` with the actual immutable release id, source hash and CLI version. Unchanged deploy keeps its id; existing snapshots never change. Choose a new positive-numbered versions/vN; saving cannot combine with --check. Saved-source deploy uses its own fixtures. Use the recorded id when choosing a baseline; since_release remains the author-supplied newer-schema label. The bridge starts from complete current Quarry source and this app's `bridge/ROLLBACK-CHECKLIST.md`. Retain current domains, audiences, guards and quotas; repair records through authorized mutations; prove blocked cases refuse and affected lists/counts remain correct; repeat the old-source preview. No bridge is activated or business migration synthesized. JSON includes checklist; --verbose retains full evidence/index paths. Dormant rows reserve ids but no unique slots: new active rows may block restoration. A widened non-unique bridge permits repair before reinstating uniqueness; retained storage cannot supply absent safety logic.
151: 
152: ## Keep v1 clients working
153: 
154: `deploy --check --json` reports `clientCompatibility` separately from data
155: adoption: missing operations, changed kinds, required new arguments, removed or
156: narrowed inputs, result shape/value changes and changed refusal codes/messages.
157: Deploy prints the same warnings. Input breaks name the old release and the
158: E_INPUT/E_OP outcome. A result or behavior review asks you to verify the intended
159: contract; a correct new privacy denial is intentional behavior, not an input
160: shape failure. The comparison is conservative, not a proof of unchanged logic.
161: 
162: For example, save this v1 source and its callers sending
163: `{name:"Ada",obsolete:"old input"}` to `greet`:
164: 
165: ```quarry
166: use identity guests
167: query greet(name: text <=40, obsolete: text <=40):
168:   return {message: name + '!'}
169: ```
170: 
171: Deploy this v2 source. The unchanged v1 call still returns `{message:"Ada!"}`;
172: new callers can use `greeting` and omit `suffix` and `obsolete`:
173: 
174: ```quarry
175: use identity guests
176: query greeting(name: text <=40, suffix: text <=1 default '!', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated):
177:   return {message: name + suffix}
178: alias greet = greeting
179: ```
180: 
181: A literal default handles omitted or null arguments and must fit the declared
182: scalar type; explicit non-null input still validates. `deprecated` accepts a
183: validated optional argument but forbids referring to it in the body. Unknown
184: arguments still refuse. These modifiers apply to non-native Quarry queries and
185: mutations; helpers, actions and jobs keep their distinct invocation contracts.
186: Aliases target an authored query/mutation in the same module, carry the same
187: rules/guards/results, and cannot chain. Retain the old endpoint until its callers
188: have retired; preserve the old result projection and handle intended denials.
189: 
190: Deploy also warns when later rollback can refuse or hide rows: dropping a
191: required old column or widening a value domain can strand newly written rows.
192: The preflight separately attempts exact reverse adoption on its post-deploy
193: snapshot, naming present-data failures. Keep old fields and compatible domains
194: in an expand/contract bridge; after writes run the named
195: `rollback <deployment> --to N --check --bridge <new-dir>` before selecting N.
196: A green snapshot never freezes later writes.

493: pub fn rollback(options: &Options) -> i32 {
494:     report(
495:         "rollback",
496:         (|| {
497:             let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
498:             let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
499:             let _deployment = target(&dir)?;
500:             if let Some(owner) = &_deployment { owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
501:             let held = status(&dir, _deployment.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?;
502:             let active = if let Some(owner) = _deployment.as_ref() {
503:                 current(&dir, owner)?
504:             } else {
505:                 held["release"].as_u64()
506:             }
507:             .ok_or("no current release")?;
508:             let revision = held["revision"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
509:             let selected = options
510:                 .flags
511:                 .get("to")
512:                 .map(|n| {
513:                     if n=="latest" {return latest(&dir).map_err(|_| "no complete latest release");}
514:                     n.parse::<u64>()
515:                         .ok()
516:                         .filter(|n| *n > 0)
517:                         .ok_or("--to requires a positive immutable release id")
518:                 })
519:                 .transpose()?;
520:             let previous = selected
521:                 .or_else(|| held["previous_active"].as_u64())
522:                 .ok_or("nothing to roll back to")?;
523:             selectable(&dir, previous)?;
524:             let bound = bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(previous.to_string()))?;
525:             // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#14-wave-14-rollback-narration — resolve bare/latest selection visibly before any preview or live activation.
526:             let target_line=format!("resolved rollback target: release {previous} (current release {active})");
527:             if options.json {eprintln!("{target_line}");} else {println!("{target_line}");}
528:             check_change(&dir, Some(active), &bound, options, Some(previous))?;
529:             if options.flags.contains_key("check") {
530:                 return super::release_preview::preview(&dir.join("store.sqlite"),dir.join("releases").join(previous.to_string()),previous,options);
531:             }
532:             activate(
533:                 &dir,
534:                 _deployment.as_ref(),
535:                 previous,
536:                 Some(active),
537:                 revision,
538:                 bound,
539:                 true,
540:                 true,
541:                 options.flags.contains_key("allow-feed-adoption"),
542:                 options,
543:                 None,
544:             )?;
545: 
546:             Ok(())
547:         })(),
548:     )
549: }
550: 
551: pub fn releases(options: &Options) -> i32 {
552:     report(
553:         "releases",
554:         (|| {
555:             let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
556:             let after = options
557:                 .flags
558:                 .get("after")
559:                 .map(|s| s.parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| "--after requires an event id"))
560:                 .transpose()?
561:                 .unwrap_or(0);
562:             let event = options
563:                 .flags
564:                 .get("event")
565:                 .map(|s| s.parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| "--event requires an event id"))
566:                 .transpose()?;
567:             // Read-only inspection needs no ownership handoff or source adoption.
568:             let mut store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))
569:                 .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
570:             let mut result = store
571:                 .release_status(
572:                     after,
573:                     options.flags.get("items-after").map(String::as_str),
574:                     event,
575:                 )
576:                 .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
577:             if result["release"].is_null() { result["release"]=serde_json::json!(inspect_current(&dir)?); }
578:             result["current"]=result["release"].clone();
579:             result["latest"]=serde_json::json!(latest(&dir).ok());
580:             if let Some(selector)=options.positionals.get(1) {
581:                 let n=if selector=="latest" {latest(&dir)?} else if selector=="current" {result["release"].as_u64().ok_or("no current release")?} else {selector.parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or("releases selection requires N, latest or current")?};
582:                 selectable(&dir,n)?;let root=dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string());bound_at(root.clone())?;
583:                 result["selected"]=serde_json::json!({"release":n,"current":result["release"].as_u64()==Some(n),"digest":crate::release_files::digest(&root)?});
584:             }
585:             println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&result).unwrap());
586:             Ok(())
587:         })(),
588:     )
589: }

78: pub struct Bound {
79:     pub invariants: Vec<security::Invariant>,
80:     /// Receipt member operation names, needed only by TypeScript emission.
81:     pub receipt_members: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
82:     /// Compiler-side source map; never serialized into the client backend.
83:     pub refusal_sites: std::collections::BTreeMap<(String, String), String>,
84:     pub view_types: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, crate::types::Ty>,
85:     /// Authoring-only opt-in; never stored in the core schema, IR or wire.
86:     pub server: bool,
87:     pub schema: Schema,
88:     pub programs: Vec<BoundProgram>,
89:     pub personas: Vec<String>,
90:     pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
91:     /// Facts the compiler can state that are not errors: a page that is
92:     /// never `complete` because its read rule is not decided by its prefix.
93:     pub notes: Vec<Diagnostic>,
94: }
95: 
96: pub fn bind(documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
97:     let mut bound = bind_unverified(documents.clone());
98:     crate::repair::verify_bound(&documents, &mut bound.diagnostics);
99:     bound
100: }
101: 
102: // Repair checks run the same planner and admission, without recursively
103: // verifying the diagnostics from a rejected candidate.
104: pub(crate) fn bind_unverified(mut documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
105:     let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
106:     operation_evolution::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
107:     let mut first_identity = None;
108:     for document in &documents {
109:         if let (Some(methods), Some(site)) = (document.identity, document.identity_site.as_ref()) {
110:             if let Some((first_methods, first_site)) = &first_identity {
111:                 diagnostics.push(crate::parser::repeated_identity(
112:                     site,
113:                     *first_methods,
114:                     first_site,
115:                     methods,
116:                 ));
117:             } else {
118:                 first_identity = Some((methods, site.clone()));
119:             }
120:         }
121:     }
122:     // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — catalog provenance comes from source, before any generated declarations.
123:     let authored_indexes = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc|
124:         doc.tables.iter().map(|t| (&t.name, &t.indexes))
125:             .chain(doc.maintains.iter().map(|m| (&m.name, &m.indexes))))
126:         .map(|(name, indexes)| (name.clone(), indexes.iter().map(|i| i.name.clone()).collect())).collect();
127:     receipts::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
128:     ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics);
129:     helper_names::normalize(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
130:     let server = documents.iter().any(|doc| doc.server);
131:     server::admit(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
132:     let feeds = feed_view::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
133:     // Following expansion adds its durable timeline inputs. Recheck before lowering.
134:     if !feeds.is_empty() { ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics); }
135:     let documents = timeline::expand(documents, &mut diagnostics);
136:     let personas: Vec<String> = documents.iter().flat_map(|d| d.personas.iter().cloned()).collect();
137:     let mut catalog = Catalog::build(documents, &mut diagnostics);
138:     catalog.feed_views = feeds;
139:     page_shapes::hoist(&mut catalog);
140:     mixed_keys::normalize(&mut catalog);
141:     intent::table_order(&mut catalog);
142:     intent_aggregates::remember_domains(&mut catalog);
143:     intent_search::capture(&mut catalog);
144:     intent_variants::expand(&mut catalog);
145:     intent_views::expand(&mut catalog, &mut diagnostics);
146:     // After argument-presence variants: `x = null or …` stays theirs (cursor validation across variants).
147:     where_guards::lower(&mut catalog);
148:     let stable_fields = catalog.tables.iter().map(|(name, table)| {
149:         let mut fields: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = table.decl.columns.iter()
150:             .filter(|column| column.written_by.is_none() && column.maintained.is_none()).map(|column| column.name.clone()).collect();
151:         fields.insert("id".into()); (name.clone(), fields)
152:     }).collect();
153:     for doc in &mut catalog.documents { for program in &mut doc.programs { stable_page_keys::lower_program(program, &stable_fields); } }
154:     intent::plan(&mut catalog, &mut diagnostics);
155:     reference_actions::indexes(&mut catalog);
156:     let mut schema = schema::bind_schema(&catalog, &mut diagnostics);
157:     schema.authored_indexes = snapback4_core::schema::inspection::AuthoredIndexes(Some(authored_indexes));
158:     timeline::admit(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
159:     delivery::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
160:     receipts::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
161:     schema.prepare_receipt_dependencies();
162:     reference_actions::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
163:     // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — timeline mappings exist only after lowering both spellings.
164:     for issue in snapback4_core::schema::field_authority::validate(&schema) {
165:         if !diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY" && d.message == issue.message) {
166:             diagnostics.push(crate::diag::Diagnostic::new(&catalog.site_for(&issue.path), "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", issue.message,
167:                 "declare readable by audience before using a private field in a timeline mapping or predicate").guide("rules"));
168:         }
169:     }
170:     // Timeline ownership is resolved after the ordinary schema checks.
171:     for issue in snapback4_core::schema::columns::timeline_issues(&schema) {
172:         diagnostics.push(columns::refusal(&catalog.site_for(&issue.path), issue.message));
173:     }
174:     key_bound::check(&catalog, &schema, &mut diagnostics);
175:     let mut view_types: std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.feed_views.iter().map(|(name, plan)| (name.clone(), plan.ty())).collect();
176:     for doc in &catalog.documents {
177:         for decl in doc.programs.iter().filter(|decl| decl.name.starts_with("$view:")) {
178:             if let Some(bound) = program::Binder::bind(&catalog, &schema, "schema", decl, &mut diagnostics) {
179:                 view_types.extend(bound.view_types);
180:             }

1: //! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
2: use super::Parser;
3: use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
4: impl Parser {
5:     pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
6:         self.take("(")?;
7:         let mut args = Vec::new();
8:         while !self.at(")") {
9:             let name = self.peek().clone();
10:             let binding = self.local_name()?;
11:             self.take(":")?;
12:             let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
13:             let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
14:                 if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
15:                     // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
16:                     if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
17:                     else { d.mechanical = false; }
18:                 }
19:                 d
20:             })?;
21:             let default = if self.maybe("default") { Some(self.expression(0, &[",", ")", "deprecated"])? ) } else { None };
22:             let deprecated = self.maybe("deprecated");
23:             if self.at("=") { return Err(syntax(&self.peek().site,"argument defaults use `default <literal>`")); }
24:             args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, default, deprecated, site: name.site });
25:             if !self.maybe(",") {
26:                 break;
27:             }
28:         }
29:         self.take(")")?;
30:         Ok(args)
31:     }
32: 
33:     pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
34:         let site = self.peek().site.clone();
35:         let mut kind;
36:         if self.maybe("[") {
37:             let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
38:             self.take("]")?;
39:             // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
40:             let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
41:             let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
42:                 if parser.at("-") {
43:                     return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
44:                 }
45:                 if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
46:                 let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
47:                 if token.value.contains('.') {
48:                     return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
49:                 }
50:                 token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
51:             };
52:             let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
53:                 (0, number(self)?)
54:             } else {
55:                 let min = number(self)?;
56:                 if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
57:                 (min, number(self)?)
58:             };
59:             if min > max {
60:                 return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
61:             }
62:             if item.optional {
63:                 return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
64:             }
65:             kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
66:         } else {
67:             let name = self.word()?;
68:             kind = match name.value.as_str() {
69:                 "text" => {
70:                     let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
71:                         (None, self.number()?)
72:                     } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
73:                         let min = self.number()?;
74:                         self.take(".")?;
75:                         self.take(".")?;
76:                         (Some(min), self.number()?)
77:                     } else {
78:                         // The default is normalized here; every downstream
79:                         // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
80:                         (None, 10_000)
81:                     };
82:                     let mut format = None;
83:                     if self.maybe("format") {
84:                         let value = self.word()?;
85:                         if !matches!(value.value.as_str(), "handle" | "url" | "email") {
86:                             let bound = match min {
87:                                 Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
88:                                 None => format!("<={max}"),
89:                             };
90:                             return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle, text {bound} format url, or text {bound} format email")));
91:                         }
92:                         format = Some(value.value);
93:                     }
94:                     TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
95:                 }
96:                 "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
97:                 "money" => {
98:                     let currency = self.word()?;
99:                     if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
100:                         return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));

229:         };
230:         // Establish cleanup custody before any fallible setup after bind.
231:         fs::set_permissions(&owned.path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600))
232:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
233:         owned
234:             .listener
235:             .set_nonblocking(true)
236:             .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
237:         socket_entry(&owned.path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
238:         Ok(owned)
239:     }
240:     fn accept(&self) -> io::Result<UnixStream> {
241:         let (stream, _) = self.listener.accept()?;
242:         // Darwin inherits O_NONBLOCK from the listener; input's deadline and
243:         // socket timeouts require blocking I/O, even before the client writes.
244:         stream.set_nonblocking(false)?;
245:         Ok(stream)
246:     }
247:     fn handle(
248:         &self,
249:         stream: &mut UnixStream,
250:         mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>,
251:     ) {
252:         let result = (|| -> Result<Value, String> {
253:             peer(&stream).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
254:             stream
255:                 .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
256:                 .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
257:             let bytes = input(stream, Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
258:             if bytes.len() > INPUT {
259:                 return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
260:             }
261:             let request: Envelope = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
262:             if request.identity != self.identity {
263:                 return Err("release control store identity differs; run releases against the owned deployment".into());
264:             }
265:             handle(request.command)
266:         })();
267:         let reply = match result {
268:             Ok(value) => json!({"ok":value}),
269:             Err(error) => json!({"error":error}),
270:         };
271:         let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&reply).unwrap();
272:         if bytes.len() > OUTPUT {
273:             bytes=serde_json::to_vec(&json!({"error":"release control reply exceeds its bound; inspect paginated `snapback4 releases <dir>`; an admitted activation/prune may already have committed"})).unwrap();
274:         }
275:         // A timeout/lost peer does not cancel or undo admitted work.
276:         let _ = stream.write_all(&bytes);
277:     }
278:     pub(crate) fn run(self, mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>) {
279:         while !crate::lifecycle::stopping() {
280:             match self.accept() {
281:                 Ok(mut stream) => {
282:                     self.handle(&mut stream, &mut handle);
283:                 }
284:                 Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock => {
285:                     std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(25))
286:                 }
287:                 Err(e) => {
288:                     snapback4_core::diagnostic!("release control accept: {e}");
289:                     std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
290:                 }
291:             }
292:         }
293:     }
294: }
295: #[cfg(unix)]
296: impl Drop for Listener {
297:     fn drop(&mut self) {
298:         if fs::symlink_metadata(&self.path)
299:             .is_ok_and(|m| m.file_type().is_socket() && (m.dev(), m.ino()) == self.inode)
300:         {
301:             let _ = fs::remove_file(&self.path);
302:         }
303:     }
304: }
305: 
306: #[cfg(unix)]
307: pub(crate) fn request(dir: &Path, command: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
308:     request_observed(dir,command, |_|{})
309: }
310: 
311: /// Submission remains in flight after a lost reply, even before history shows a commit.
312: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — source cleanup needs either no submission or a definitive completed reply.
313: #[cfg(unix)]
314: pub(crate) fn request_observed(dir: &Path, command: Command, mut pending: impl FnMut(bool)) -> Result<Value, String> {
315:     let store = dir.join("store.sqlite");
316:     let unavailable = |reason: String| {
317:         let owner_path = store.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| store.clone());
318:         let detail = if reason.contains("No such file or directory") {
319:             "this deployment has no release control socket".to_owned()
320:         } else {
321:             reason
322:         };
323:         format!(
324:             "{}; release control unavailable: {detail}; stop `snapback4 serve {}`, rerun the exact command, then `snapback4 serve {}`",
325:             crate::store_owner::busy(&owner_path),
326:             dir.display(),
327:             dir.display()
328:         )
329:     };
330:     let path = address(dir, false).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
331:     let before = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
332:     let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
333:     peer(&stream).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
334:     let after = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
335:     if (before.dev(), before.ino()) != (after.dev(), after.ino()) {
336:         return Err(unavailable(
337:             "release control socket changed before send".into(),
338:         ));
339:     }
340:     stream
341:         .set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(120)))
342:         .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
343:     stream
344:         .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
345:         .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
346:     let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&Envelope {
347:         identity: identity(&store)?,
348:         command,
349:     })
350:     .unwrap();
351:     if bytes.len() > INPUT {
352:         return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
353:     }
354:     let ambiguous = |reason: io::Error| {
355:         format!(
356:             "release command result is unknown: {reason}; run `snapback4 releases {}` to inspect database authority; the server continues admitted work. Do not automatically deploy another release",
357:             dir.display()
358:         )
359:     };
360:     pending(true);
361:     stream.write_all(&bytes).map_err(ambiguous)?;
362:     stream
363:         .shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write)
364:         .map_err(ambiguous)?;
365:     let mut reply = Vec::new();
366:     stream
367:         .take((OUTPUT + 1) as u64)
368:         .read_to_end(&mut reply)
369:         .map_err(ambiguous)?;
370:     if reply.len() > OUTPUT {
371:         return Err("release reply too large; inspect releases before retrying".into());
372:     }
373:     let value: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&reply).map_err(|e| {
374:         format!(
375:             "release result is unknown ({e}); inspect `snapback4 releases {}` before retrying",
376:             dir.display()
377:         )
378:     })?;
379:     match (value.get("ok"),value.get("error").and_then(Value::as_str)) {
380:         (Some(value),_)=>{pending(false);Ok(value.clone())},
381:         (_,Some(error))=>{pending(false);Err(error.into())},
382:         _=>Err("release result is unknown (invalid reply); inspect releases before retrying".into()),
383:     }
384: }
385: 
386: #[cfg(not(unix))]
387: pub(crate) fn request(_: &Path, _: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
388:     Err("local release control requires Unix".into())
389: }
390: 
391: #[cfg(not(unix))]
392: pub(crate) fn request_observed(_: &Path, _: Command, _: impl FnMut(bool)) -> Result<Value,String> {
393:     Err("local release control requires Unix".into())
394: }
395:

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..8c6f5cdcfa
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/release-transitions.test.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,11 @@
+import test from 'node:test';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { cli, startServe } from '../dist/proof.js';
+import { releaseTransitions } from '../../../scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs';
+test('release reactivation, refusal cleanup, rollback foresight and frozen v1 guide callers', {timeout:180_000}, async () => {
+  const original=process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;
+  process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN??=fileURLToPath(new URL('../../../target/debug/snapback4',import.meta.url));
+  try {await releaseTransitions({cli,startServe},tmpdir());}
+  finally {if(original===undefined)delete process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;else process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN=original;}
+});
diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..ad420a7b3a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/publication-release-transitions.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,13 @@
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — the installed guide is the source exercised by packed qualification.
+import {readFileSync, writeFileSync, rmSync} from 'node:fs';
+import {join} from 'node:path';
+import {runToolchainCommand} from './publication-starters.mjs';
+export async function proveReleaseTransitions(cwd) {
+  const path=join(cwd,'release-transitions.mjs');
+  const source=readFileSync(new URL('./release-transitions-cases.mjs',import.meta.url),'utf8');
+  writeFileSync(path,source+`\nimport {cli,startServe} from 'snapback4/proof';\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(await releaseTransitions({cli,startServe},process.cwd())));\n`);
+  const env={...process.env};delete env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;delete env.PORT_LO;delete env.PORT_HI;
+  try {await runToolchainCommand(process.execPath,[path],{cwd,env,timeout:240_000});}
+  finally {rmSync(path,{force:true});}
+  return {status:'passed',cases:['reactivation','refused-deploy-cleanliness','rollback-foresight','old-client-diff','installed-v1-v2-guide']};
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..15ed211030
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/release-transitions-cases.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,128 @@
+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — run the same transitions with development and installed binaries.
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, readdirSync, mkdtempSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { join } from 'node:path';
+
+export async function releaseTransitions({ cli, startServe }, parent) {
+  const root=mkdtempSync(join(parent,'release-transitions-'));
+  const active=new Set();
+  try {
+    const fixture=name=> {
+      const cwd=join(root,name);mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'),{recursive:true});
+      const deployment=join(cwd,'deployment'),schema=join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q');
+      const invoke=args=>cli(args,{cwd,timeoutMs:60_000});
+      const run=async args=>{const r=await invoke(args);assert.equal(r.code,0,r.stdout+r.stderr);return r;};
+      const status=async()=>JSON.parse((await run(['releases',deployment,'--json'])).stdout);
+      const releases=()=>readdirSync(join(deployment,'releases')).sort();
+      const source=text=>writeFileSync(schema,text);
+      const serve=async()=> {
+        const host=await startServe(deployment,{cwd,timeoutMs:60_000});active.add(host);
+        const signed=await (await fetch(host.url+'/auth/guest',{method:'POST',body:'{}'})).json();
+        assert.ok(signed.session?.token,JSON.stringify(signed));
+        const headers={'content-type':'application/json',authorization:'Bearer '+signed.session.token};
+        const post=async(path,args={},id)=> {
+          const r=await fetch(host.url+path,{method:'POST',headers,body:JSON.stringify({args,...(id?{id}:{})}),signal:AbortSignal.timeout(30_000)});
+          return r.json();
+        };
+        return {host,post};
+      };
+      return {cwd,deployment,schema,invoke,run,status,releases,source,serve};
+    };
+    const f=fixture('reactivation');
+    const v1=`use identity guests
+table items:
+  body: text <=40
+  public 'release proof'
+  insert <- allow
+  update <- allow
+mutation add(body: text <=40):
+  return insert items {body}
+query list():
+  return items first 20 by .id
+`;
+    const v2=v1.replace('  body: text','  extra: text <=40 default \'default\'\n  body: text')+`mutation mark(id: items):
+  return update items[id] {extra: 'preserved'}
+`;
+    f.source(v1);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
+    f.source(v2);await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1']);
+    const before=await f.status();
+    const check=JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
+    assert.equal(check.plan.release,2);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),before);assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);
+    assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).release,2);
+    const {host,post}=await f.serve();
+    const added=await post('/m/add',{body:'kept'},'rel5-add');assert.equal(added.state,'sent',JSON.stringify(added));
+    const id=added.result.id;
+    assert.equal((await post('/m/mark',{id},'rel5-mark')).state,'sent');
+    await f.run(['rollback',f.deployment,'--to','1','--restore-dormant']);
+    await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment]);
+    assert.equal((await f.status()).release,2);assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
+    const unchanged=await f.status();assert.equal(JSON.parse((await f.run(['deploy',f.deployment,'--json'])).stdout).unchanged,true);
+    assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
+    f.source(v2.replace('  body: text','  required: int\n  body: text').replace('insert items {body}','insert items {body, required: 1}'));
+    const failed=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(failed.code,1);assert.match(failed.stderr,/E_ADOPT/);
+    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);assert.deepEqual(await f.status(),unchanged);
+    assert.equal((await post('/q/list')).data[0].extra,'preserved');
+    await host.stop();active.delete(host);
+    // Failure after validation, before database commit, also cleans its numeric source.
+    f.source(v2.replace("return items first 20", "return items first 19"));
+    rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));mkdirSync(join(f.deployment,'current'));
+    const pointerFailure=await f.invoke(['deploy',f.deployment]);assert.equal(pointerFailure.code,1);assert.match(pointerFailure.stderr,/current must be a regular/);
+    assert.deepEqual(f.releases(),['1','2']);rmSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(f.deployment,'current'),'2\n');
+
+    const g=fixture('foresight');
+    const prior=`use identity guests
+table gauges:
+  minPressure: int 0..2000
+  public 'rollback proof'
+  insert <- allow
+mutation add():
+  return insert gauges {minPressure: 4}
+`;
+    const next=prior.replace('  minPressure: int 0..2000\n','  lowPressure: int 0..2000 default 0\n').replace('{minPressure: 4}','{lowPressure: 4}');
+    g.source(prior);await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment]);g.source(next);
+    const foresight=JSON.parse((await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);
+    assert.ok(foresight.hazards.some(s=>s.includes('gauges.minPressure')&&s.includes('new rows')&&s.includes('--to 1 --check --bridge')));
+    assert.match((await g.run(['deploy',g.deployment])).stderr,/rollback foresight.*gauges.minPressure/);
+    const h=await g.serve();assert.equal((await h.post('/m/add',{},'rel5-new-gauge')).state,'sent');
+    const rollback=await g.invoke(['rollback',g.deployment,'--to','1','--check']);assert.equal(rollback.code,1);assert.match(rollback.stderr,/minPressure/);
+    await h.host.stop();active.delete(h.host);
+
+    const c=fixture('contracts');
+    // Execute both Quarry blocks from the installed guide, without source substitutions.
+    const guide=(await c.run(['guide','deploy','--section','Keep v1 clients working'])).stdout;
+    const blocks=[...guide.matchAll(/```quarry\n([\s\S]*?)```/g)].map(m=>m[1]);assert.equal(blocks.length,2,guide);
+    c.source(blocks[0]);await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment]);
+    const oldHost=await c.serve();const frozenArgs={name:'Ada',obsolete:'old input'};
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greet',frozenArgs)).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    c.source(blocks[1]);
+    const safe=JSON.parse((await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout);assert.deepEqual(safe.clientCompatibility,[]);
+    await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment]);
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greet',frozenArgs)).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    assert.deepEqual((await oldHost.post('/q/greeting',{name:'Ada'})).data,{message:'Ada!'});
+    await oldHost.host.stop();active.delete(oldHost.host);
+    const unsafe=blocks[1].replace("suffix: text <=1 default '!'","suffix: text <=1").replace(', obsolete: text <=40 deprecated','');
+    c.source(unsafe);
+    const diff=JSON.parse((await c.run(['deploy',c.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
+    assert.match(diff,/greet.*argument suffix retyped or narrowed/);assert.match(diff,/greet.*removed argument obsolete/);
+    mkdirSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1'),{recursive:true});writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'versions/v1/schema.q'),blocks[0]);
+    writeFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/client-compatibility.json'),JSON.stringify({baseline:'versions/v1',since_release:2}));
+    const baseline=await c.run(['check']);assert.match(baseline.stderr,/old-client compatibility \(baseline .*new required argument suffix/);
+    const generated=readFileSync(join(c.cwd,'snapback/generated/api.ts'),'utf8');assert.match(generated,/suffix: string/);assert.doesNotMatch(generated,/suffix\?:/);
+    const d=fixture('projected-domains');
+    const rows=`use identity guests
+table items:
+  n: int 0..10
+  label: text 1..20
+  public 'result domain proof'
+query list():
+  return items first 10 by .id {n, label}
+`;
+    d.source(rows);await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment]);
+    d.source(rows.replace('int 0..10','int 0..20').replace('text 1..20','text 0..20'));
+    const domains=JSON.parse((await d.run(['deploy',d.deployment,'--check','--json'])).stdout).clientCompatibility.join('\n');
+    assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.label/);assert.match(domains,/RESULT REVIEW.*items.n/);
+    return {status:'passed',reactivation:['cold','hot','same-id','dormant-values','unchanged'],refusals:['adoption','post-validation-pointer','no-candidate-left'],rollbackForesight:'dropped-required-column-and-later-writes',oldClients:'installed-guide-signature-diff-baseline-projected-domains'};
+  } finally {
+    try {for(const host of active)await host.stop();} finally {rmSync(root,{recursive:true,force:true});}
+  }
