codex
The candidate cleanup paths distinguish unsent requests, definitive replies and submissions that may still commit. The tests cover retaining source after a lost reply and restarting from it. I’m now checking language and warning coverage for gaps within the amended contract.

35: pub(super) fn compatibility(store: &Store, current: &Schema, target: &Schema, current_release: u64, target_label: &str, include_future: bool) -> Result<Vec<String>, String> {
36:     let mut lines = Vec::new();
37:     for path in narrower(current, target) {
38:         let (name, column) = path.split_once('.').unwrap();
39:         let (count, values) = store.rollback_incompatible_values(current, target, name, column).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
40:         if count > 0 {
41:             lines.push(format!("WARNING {path}: {count} {} {} {}, which {target_label} cannot hold; {} dormant on this switch and {} restored on compatible forward; accept their absence or preserve these values in a bridge release",
42:                 if count == 1 { "row" } else { "rows" }, if count == 1 { "holds" } else { "hold" }, values.join(", "),
43:                 if count == 1 { "it becomes" } else { "they become" }, if count == 1 { "is" } else { "are" }));
44:         } else {
45:             lines.push(format!("note: {path}: no stored values are incompatible with {target_label}; no value repair is needed for this switch"));
46:         }
47:     }
48:     for path in if include_future { narrower(target, current) } else { BTreeSet::new() } {
49:         let (name, column) = path.split_once('.').unwrap();
50:         let wide = &target.tables[name].columns[column];
51:         let narrow = &current.tables[name].columns[column];
52:         let admission = match (wide, narrow) {
53:             (snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum { variants }, snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum { variants: old }) =>
54:                 variants.iter().filter(|v| !old.contains(v)).map(|v| snapback4_lang::print::quote(v)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "),
55:             _ => format!("values outside release {current_release}'s column declaration"),
56:         };
57:         lines.push(format!("note: {path} admits {admission}; rows that later use those values become dormant on rollback to release {current_release}; keep this value domain in a bridge if those rows must stay available"));
58:     }
59:     Ok(lines)
60: }
61: 
62: // The lexer excludes comments and positions while retaining indentation and literals.
63: // Compare authored declarations, not compiler-inserted default/column plumbing.
64: fn declarations(project: &Project) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, Vec<(Kind, String)>>, String> {
65:     let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
66:     for (file, source) in project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
67:         let (document, _) = snapback4_lang::parse_document(&file, &source);
68:         let (tokens, errors) = lexer::lex(&file, &source);
69:         if !errors.is_empty() { return Err(format!("cannot compare job source in {file}: lexical diagnostics")); }
70:         let mut roots = Vec::new();
71:         let (mut indent, mut groups) = (0usize, 0usize);
72:         for (index, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate() {
73:             // Continuation fields/arguments can start at column one inside
74:             // delimiters. Only the lexer's actual root declarations end a job.
75:             if indent == 0 && groups == 0 && token.kind == Kind::Word && token.site.col == 1 {
76:                 roots.push((token.site.line, index));
77:             }
78:             match token.kind {
79:                 Kind::Indent => indent += 1,
80:                 Kind::Dedent => indent = indent.saturating_sub(1),
81:                 Kind::Symbol if matches!(token.value.as_str(), "(" | "[" | "{") => groups += 1,
82:                 Kind::Symbol if matches!(token.value.as_str(), ")" | "]" | "}") => groups = groups.saturating_sub(1),
83:                 _ => (),
84:             }
85:         }
86:         for program in document.programs {
87:             let Some(position) = roots.iter().position(|(line, _)| *line == program.site.line) else { continue; };
88:             let start = roots[position].1;
89:             let end = roots.get(position + 1).map_or(tokens.len(), |(_, index)| *index);
90:             let module = Path::new(&file).file_stem().and_then(|s| s.to_str()).unwrap_or("schema");
91:             let name = if module == "schema" { program.name } else { format!("{module}.{}", program.name) };
92:             out.insert(name, tokens[start..end].iter().filter(|t| t.kind != Kind::Eof).map(|t| (t.kind, t.value.clone())).collect());
93:         }
94:     }
95:     Ok(out)
96: }

392: impl Scan {
393:     /// True only for an ordinary index ordered by this column after its pins.
394:     /// Kept shared by execution and the cost certificate.
395:     pub fn orders_field(&self, schema: &crate::schema::Schema, field: &str) -> bool {
396:         let Some(index) = schema.table(&self.table).and_then(|table| table.index(&self.index)) else { return false; };
397:         // Expiring maintained totals are refreshed while reading. Their
398:         // stored index value does not order the live value returned today.
399:         if field == "value" && schema.maintain_for(&self.table).is_some_and(|maintain|
400:             schema.table(&maintain.source).is_some_and(|source| source.expire.is_some())) { return false; }
401:         self.prefix.iter().all(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(_)))
402:             && matches!(index.components.get(self.prefix.len()), Some(crate::schema::IndexComponent::Column(column)) if column == field)
403:     }
404: }

448: /// Query lane domains reuse statement-loop binding and evaluation. The scan
449: /// variant retains its wire shape for the frozen conformance programs.

451: #[serde(untagged)]
452: pub enum LaneSource {
453:     Scan(Scan),
454:     Range { start: Expr, end_exclusive: Expr, max: Option<u64> },
455:     List { value: Expr, max: u32 },
456: }

458: mod lane_source;
459: 
460: impl LaneSource {
461:     pub fn scan(&self) -> Option<&Scan> { if let Self::Scan(scan) = self { Some(scan) } else { None } }
462:     pub fn expressions(&self) -> Vec<&Expr> {
463:         match self { Self::Scan(_) => Vec::new(), Self::Range { start, end_exclusive, .. } => vec![start, end_exclusive], Self::List { value, .. } => vec![value] }
464:     }
465:     pub fn static_max(&self) -> Option<u64> {
466:         match self {
467:             Self::Scan(scan) => if scan.take == Bound::Meter { None } else { Some(scan.take.maximum()) },
468:             Self::Range { max, .. } => *max,
469:             Self::List { max, .. } => Some(u64::from(*max)),
470:         }
471:     }
472:     pub fn domain(&self) -> LoopDomain {
473:         match self {
474:             Self::Scan(scan) => LoopDomain::Page(scan.clone()),
475:             Self::Range { start, end_exclusive, .. } => LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start: start.clone(), end_exclusive: end_exclusive.clone() },
476:             Self::List { value, max } => LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value: value.clone(), max: *max, bound_source: "list".into() },
477:         }
478:     }
479: }

481: impl Merge {
482:     pub fn take(&self) -> u64 {
483:         self.position.as_ref().map_or_else(|| self.lanes.first().map_or(0, |lane| lane.scan.take.maximum()), |position| u64::from(position.take))
484:     }
485:     pub fn table(&self) -> &str {
486:         self.lanes.first().map_or("", |lane| lane.scan.table.as_str())
487:     }
488:     pub fn max_lanes(&self) -> u64 {
489:         self.static_lanes().unwrap_or(crate::meter::Ceilings::default().examined)
490:     }
491:     pub fn static_lanes(&self) -> Option<u64> {
492:         self.lanes.iter().try_fold(0u64, |sum, lane| Some(sum.saturating_add(match &lane.source { None => 1, Some(source) => source.static_max()? })))
493:     }
494:     /// A branch merge: a query page planned as the k-way merge of its
495:     /// disjunctive branches, each lane an index range of one table whose
496:     /// prefix decides the read rule (or a grant lane). Its lanes carry
497:     /// one of the branch identities below; no view does.
498:     // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#3 — the lane identity marks the plan, so no existing program's IR or digest changes.
499:     pub fn branches(&self) -> bool {
500:         self.view.is_none() && !self.lanes.is_empty()
501:             && self.lanes.iter().all(|lane| matches!(lane.branch.as_deref(), Some(BRANCH_LANE | GRANT_LANE | MEMBERSHIP_LANE | SYSTEM_LANE | GUARDED_LANE | VACANCY_LANE | BOUNDED_LANE)))
502:     }
503:     /// The cursor descriptor's index field: a bounded digest of the branch
504:     /// set, in lane order. Legal labels repeated across eight lanes can
505:     /// otherwise exceed the token bound before its position is encoded.
506:     // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#5 — both plan identity and pin scope have bounded digests
507:     pub fn branch_descriptor(&self) -> String {
508:         use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};
509:         let mut hash = Sha256::new();
510:         hash.update(b"branch-merge-v1\0");
511:         hash.update((self.lanes.len() as u64).to_be_bytes());
512:         for lane in &self.lanes {
513:             hash.update((lane.scan.index.len() as u64).to_be_bytes());
514:             hash.update(lane.scan.index.as_bytes());
515:             // Keep the published static branch-merge-v1 bytes in their
516:             // original order. New PAGE identity is appended only for its new
517:             // descriptors, so an existing static cursor keeps resuming.
518:             hash.update([u8::from(lane.grant())]);
519:             if lane.membership() {
520:                 hash.update(MEMBERSHIP_LANE.as_bytes());
521:             } else if lane.system() {
522:                 hash.update(SYSTEM_LANE.as_bytes());
523:             } else if lane.guarded() {
524:                 hash.update(GUARDED_LANE.as_bytes());
525:             } else if lane.bounded() {

//! Typed admission of rows and arguments at the store's trust boundary. A
//! value that does not fit its declared type is refused before any rule or
//! constraint sees it.

use crate::error::RuntimeError;
use crate::ir::{ArgType, Program};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Constraint, Schema, TextFormat, contains_system_principal};
use crate::value::{I53_MAX, I53_MIN, Value, canonical_json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub fn validate_row(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, false)
}

/// Adoption builders still own recorded/captured slots. Check authoritative
/// fields before their work, and validate the complete image afterward.
#[cfg(feature="sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_adoption_input(schema:&Schema,name:&str,row:&Value)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let table=&schema.tables[name];
    for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
        if !table.engine_field(column) {validate_column(name,column,kind,row.field(column).ok())?;}
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text{field,min,format}=constraint && !table.engine_field(field) && let Ok(Value::String(text))=row.field(field){validate_text(&format!("{name}.{field}"),text,*min,*format)?;}
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — row-view storage is a private numerical cache, not a disclosure.
// No certificate yet means no computed fields; public/input validation stays strict.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(crate) fn validate_maintained_cache(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let cache = schema.maintain_for(table_name).is_some_and(|m| m.row_key.is_some());
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, false, cache)
}

/// A predicting patch preserves unknown, untouched fields as omissions.
/// The authoritative writer always uses the complete-row validator.
pub(super) fn validate_projection(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, viewer, false, false)
}

/// Application input is checked before engine slots are prepared. The final
/// stored image must also pass validate_row, with every required slot present.
pub(super) fn validate_before_slots(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    validate_image(schema, table_name, row, None, true, false)
}

fn validate_image(schema: &Schema, table_name: &str, row: &Value, viewer: Option<&str>, before_slots: bool, cache: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let table = schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
    let object = row.as_object()?;
    if !object.contains_key("id") {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name} row lacks id")));
    }
    if let Some(name) = object.keys().find(|name| !table.columns.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} row contains undeclared field {name}")));
    }
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if before_slots && crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(name) { continue; }
        if cache && matches!(name.as_str(), "value" | "recountPending") && !object.contains_key(name) { continue; }
        let value = object.get(name).or_else(|| table.maintained.contains_key(name).then_some(&Value::Int(0)));
        if value.is_none() && viewer.is_some_and(|viewer| table.written_by.get(name).is_some_and(|authority|
            !authority.audience && row.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(viewer.into())))) { continue; }
        validate_column(table_name, name, kind, value)?;
    }
    for constraint in &table.constraints {
        if let Constraint::Text { field, min, format } = constraint
            && let Some(Value::String(text)) = object.get(field)
        {
            validate_text(&format!("{table_name}.{field}"), text, *min, *format).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Constraint(error.to_string().trim_start_matches("E_INPUT: ").to_owned()))?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_column(table_name: &str, name: &str, kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let label = format!("{table_name}.{name}");
    if let Some(value) = value {
        crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
        if contains_system_principal(value) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
        }
    }
    if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_control(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
    }
    validate_column_minimum(kind, value, &label)?;
    if !fits(kind, value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match value {
            None | Some(Value::Null) => format!("{label} is required ({kind})"),
            Some(value) => format!("{label} must be {kind}, not {}", describe(value)),
        }));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — nested/optional columns use the same minimum on server and device writes.
fn validate_column_minimum(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => validate_column_minimum(inner, Some(value), label),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(items))) => {
            if let ColumnType::ListRange { min, .. } = kind { validate_list_minimum(label, items.len(), *min)?; }
            for item in items { validate_column_minimum(of, Some(item), label)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}

fn validate_list_minimum(label: &str, length: usize, min: u32) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if length < min as usize {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} {}; got {length}", if min == 1 { "item" } else { "items" })));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn describe(value: &Value) -> String {
    match value {
        Value::String(text) if text.chars().count() > 24 => format!("{} characters of text", text.chars().count()),
        Value::String(text) => format!("the text '{text}'"),
        Value::Id(id) => format!("the id '{id}'"),
        Value::Int(n) => format!("the number {n}"),
        Value::Bool(b) => format!("the boolean {b}"),
        Value::Null => "null".into(),
        Value::Bytes(b) => format!("{} bytes", b.len()),
        Value::Array(items) => format!("a list of {}", items.len()),
        Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => "an object".into(),
    }
}

pub fn validate_text(label: &str, text: &str, min: u32, format: Option<TextFormat>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#6-bakeoff-repairs — minimums measure evidence, while maxima still measure the complete value.
    let length = text.chars().filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace()).count();
    if length < min as usize {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} needs at least {min} non-whitespace characters; got {length}")));
    }
    if let Some(format) = format
        && !format.matches(text)
    {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(match format {
            TextFormat::Handle => format!("{label} must be a handle: lowercase letters, digits and underscores"),
            TextFormat::Url => format!("{label} must be an http or https URL"),
            TextFormat::Email => format!("{label} must be an ASCII email address with one @, a dot-safe local part, and valid domain labels"),
        }));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn disallowed_control(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> Option<u32> {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => disallowed_control(inner, Some(value)),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.iter().find_map(|value| disallowed_control(of, Some(value))),
        (ColumnType::Text { .. }, Some(Value::String(text))) => disallowed_text_control(text),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Text forbids C0 controls except tab, LF and CR.
pub fn disallowed_text_control(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    text.chars().find(|c| c.is_control() && !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r')).map(|c| c as u32)
}

pub(crate) fn fits(kind: &ColumnType, value: Option<&Value>) -> bool {
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(_), None | Some(Value::Null)) => true,
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), Some(value)) => fits(inner, Some(value)),
        (ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. }, Some(Value::Array(values))) => values.len() <= *max as usize && values.iter().all(|value| fits(of, Some(value))),
        // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — typed references do not imply target existence
        (ColumnType::Id | ColumnType::Principal | ColumnType::Ref { .. }, Some(Value::Id(_))) => true,
        (ColumnType::Text { max }, Some(Value::String(text))) => text.chars().count() <= *max as usize,
        (ColumnType::Bytes { max }, Some(Value::Bytes(bytes))) => bytes.len() <= *max as usize,
        (ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. }, Some(Value::Map(fields))) => fields.get("id").is_some_and(|id| matches!(id, Value::Id(_))),
        (ColumnType::Enum { variants }, Some(Value::String(value))) => variants.contains(value),
        (ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::Time | ColumnType::Decimal { .. } | ColumnType::Money { .. }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value),
        // @ref LLP 3000.040#14-where-the-bound-is-enforced — the slot's bound is part of its type: every stored write and argument meets it here.
        (ColumnType::IntRange { min, max }, Some(Value::Int(value))) => (I53_MIN..=I53_MAX).contains(value) && (*min..=*max).contains(value),
        (ColumnType::Bool, Some(Value::Bool(_))) => true,
        (ColumnType::Json { max_bytes }, Some(value)) => canonical_json(value).len() <= *max_bytes as usize,
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Validate the arguments and fill each omitted optional with `null`, so
/// a caller that leaves `c` out reads page one.
#[cfg(not(all(target_arch = "wasm32", feature = "web", not(feature = "sqlite"))))]
pub fn validate_inputs(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    super::admission::check(schema, program)?;
    validate_arguments(schema, program, inputs)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.002#proposed-device-representation-and-codec-redesign — the web predicts a server-admitted backend; the server and native hosts still check every call.
pub(super) fn validate_device_inputs(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    #[cfg(not(any(all(target_arch = "wasm32", feature = "web", not(feature = "sqlite")), feature = "web-device-check")))]
    super::admission::check(schema, program)?;
    validate_arguments(schema, program, inputs)
}

fn validate_arguments(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, kind) in &program.args {
        if !inputs.contains_key(name) && (kind.is_optional() || matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor)) {
            inputs.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
        }
        let value = inputs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input(format!("missing argument {name}")))?;
        validate_arg(schema, kind, value, name)?;
    }
    if let Some(extra) = inputs.keys().find(|name| !program.args.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{} takes no argument {extra}", program.name)));
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_arg(schema: &Schema, kind: &ArgType, value: &Value, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !kind.bounds_valid() {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} has a minimum greater than its maximum")));
    }
    crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
    if contains_system_principal(value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
    }
    match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor => match value {
            Value::Null => Ok(()),
            Value::String(token) => crate::cursor::decode_cursor(schema, token).map(|_| ()).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label}: {error}"))),
            _ => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be an opaque cursor string or null"))),
        },
        ArgType::Optional(_) if *value == Value::Null => Ok(()),
        ArgType::Optional(inner) => validate_arg(schema, inner, value, label),
        ArgType::Scalar => match value {
            Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be a scalar"))),
            _ => Ok(()),
        },
        ArgType::Text { max, min, format } => match value {
            Value::String(text) => {
                if let Some(code_point) = disallowed_text_control(text) {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} contains disallowed control character U+{code_point:04X}")));
                }
                if text.chars().count() > *max as usize {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} is longer than {max} characters")));
                }
                validate_text(label, text, *min, *format)
            }
            other => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be text, not {}", describe(other)))),
        },
        ArgType::List { of, max } | ArgType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => match value {
            Value::Array(items) => {
                if let ArgType::ListRange { min, .. } = kind { validate_list_minimum(label, items.len(), *min)?; }
                if items.len() > *max as usize {
                    return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} has more than {max} items")));
                }
                if let Some(of) = of {
                    for item in items {
                        validate_arg(schema, of, item, label)?;
                    }
                }
                Ok(())
            }
            other => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be a list, not {}", describe(other)))),
        },
        other => {
            let column = crate::schema::arg_as_column(other).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} has no column form")))?;
            if fits(&column, Some(value)) { Ok(()) } else { Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be {column}, not {}", describe(value)))) }
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod security_minimum_tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn unicode_whitespace_does_not_supply_required_evidence() {
        for text in ["", " ", "\t\n\r", "\u{00a0}\u{2003}\u{202f}", "a \t"] {
            assert!(validate_text("reason", text, 2, None).is_err(), "{text:?}");
        }
        assert!(validate_text("reason", " a \t b\n", 2, None).is_ok());
        assert!(validate_text("optional", " \t", 0, None).is_ok());
    }
    #[test]
    fn argument_lists_and_optionals_share_the_minimum_and_maximum() {
        let text=ArgType::Text {min:2,max:8,format:None};
        let list=ArgType::List {of:Some(Box::new(ArgType::Optional(Box::new(text)))),max:2};
        let schema=Schema::default();
        assert!(validate_arg(&schema,&list,&Value::Array(vec![Value::String("  ".into())]),"reasons").is_err());
        assert!(validate_arg(&schema,&list,&Value::Array(vec![Value::Null,Value::String("ab".into())]),"reasons").is_ok());
        let text=ArgType::Text {min:2,max:3,format:None};
        assert!(validate_arg(&schema,&text,&Value::String(" ab ".into()),"reason").is_err());
    }
}

79: impl Walk<'_> {
80:     fn issue(&mut self, path: &str, label: &str) {
81:         self.issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: format!("maintained column {label} is depended on by {path}; use an ordinary column for authority, identity, lifetime or delivery, or a separate maintain for an aggregate (guide maintains)") });
82:     }

88:         if let Some(message) = self.schema.row_view_predicate_field(table, field) {
89:             self.issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message });
90:         }
91:         if self.schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t| t.maintained.contains_key(field)) { self.issue(path, &format!("{table}.{field}")); }
92:     }
93:     fn term(&mut self, table: &str, term: &RuleTerm, path: &str) {
94:         if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) = term { self.field(table, field, path); }
95:     }
96:     fn index(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, path: &str) {
97:         self.target(table, path);

106:     }

110:             RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { self.term(table,a,path); self.term(table,b,path); },

247:     fn handle(
248:         &self,
249:         stream: &mut UnixStream,
250:         mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>,
251:     ) {
252:         let result = (|| -> Result<Value, String> {
253:             peer(&stream).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
254:             stream
255:                 .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
256:                 .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
257:             let bytes = input(stream, Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
258:             if bytes.len() > INPUT {
259:                 return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
260:             }
261:             let request: Envelope = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
262:             if request.identity != self.identity {
263:                 return Err("release control store identity differs; run releases against the owned deployment".into());
264:             }
265:             handle(request.command)
266:         })();
267:         let reply = match result {
268:             Ok(value) => json!({"ok":value}),
269:             Err(error) => json!({"error":error}),
270:         };
271:         let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&reply).unwrap();
272:         if bytes.len() > OUTPUT {
273:             bytes=serde_json::to_vec(&json!({"error":"release control reply exceeds its bound; inspect paginated `snapback4 releases <dir>`; an admitted activation/prune may already have committed"})).unwrap();
274:         }
275:         // A timeout/lost peer does not cancel or undo admitted work.
276:         let _ = stream.write_all(&bytes);
277:     }
278:     pub(crate) fn run(self, mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>) {
279:         while !crate::lifecycle::stopping() {
280:             match self.accept() {
281:                 Ok(mut stream) => {
282:                     self.handle(&mut stream, &mut handle);
283:                 }
284:                 Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock => {
285:                     std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(25))
286:                 }
287:                 Err(e) => {
288:                     snapback4_core::diagnostic!("release control accept: {e}");
289:                     std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
290:                 }
291:             }
292:         }
293:     }
294: }

306: #[cfg(unix)]
307: pub(crate) fn request(dir: &Path, command: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
308:     request_observed(dir,command, |_|{})
309: }
310: 
311: /// Submission remains in flight after a lost reply, even before history shows a commit.
312: // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — source cleanup needs either no submission or a definitive completed reply.
313: #[cfg(unix)]
314: pub(crate) fn request_observed(dir: &Path, command: Command, mut pending: impl FnMut(bool)) -> Result<Value, String> {
315:     let store = dir.join("store.sqlite");
316:     let unavailable = |reason: String| {
317:         let owner_path = store.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| store.clone());
318:         let detail = if reason.contains("No such file or directory") {
319:             "this deployment has no release control socket".to_owned()
320:         } else {
321:             reason
322:         };
323:         format!(
324:             "{}; release control unavailable: {detail}; stop `snapback4 serve {}`, rerun the exact command, then `snapback4 serve {}`",
325:             crate::store_owner::busy(&owner_path),
326:             dir.display(),
327:             dir.display()
328:         )
329:     };
330:     let path = address(dir, false).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
331:     let before = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
332:     let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
333:     peer(&stream).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
334:     let after = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
335:     if (before.dev(), before.ino()) != (after.dev(), after.ino()) {
336:         return Err(unavailable(
337:             "release control socket changed before send".into(),
338:         ));
339:     }
340:     stream
341:         .set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(120)))
342:         .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
343:     stream
344:         .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
345:         .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;

snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/releases.rs
590: 
591: pub fn prune(options: &Options) -> i32 {
592:     report(
593:         "prune",
594:         (|| {
595:             let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
596:             let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
597:             let owner = target(&dir)?;
598:             let held = status(&dir, owner.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?;
599:             let revision = held["revision"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
600:             let confirm = options.flags.contains_key("allow-destructive");
601:             let result = match owner.as_ref() {
602:                 Some(owner) => owner
603:                     .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
604:                     .and_then(|mut s| s.prune_dormant(confirm, Some(revision)))
605:                     .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
606:                 None => crate::release_control::request(
607:                     &dir,
608:                     crate::release_control::Command::Prune {
609:                         confirm,
610:                         expected_revision: revision,
611:                     },
612:                 )?,
613:             };
614:             println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&result).unwrap());
615:             // Live responses are bounded. Enumerate the committed audit set (or
616:             // current preview) to name every item, never silently omit a drop.
617:             if owner.is_none() {
618:                 let mut after = result["items_after"].as_str().map(str::to_owned);
619:                 while let Some(key) = after {
620:                     let page = status(&dir, None, 0, Some(key), result["event"].as_u64())?;
621:                     let items = page["items"].as_array().ok_or("invalid inventory reply")?;
622:                     if items.is_empty() {
623:                         break;
624:                     }
625:                     println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(items).unwrap());
626:                     after = page["items_after"].as_str().map(str::to_owned);
627:                 }
628:             }
629:             if !confirm {
630:                 println!(
631:                     "preview only; to permanently drop this dormant storage: snapback4 prune {} --allow-destructive",
632:                     dir.display()
633:                 );
634:             }
635:             Ok(())
636:         })(),
637:     )
638: }
639: 
640: pub fn serve_release(options: &Options) -> Result<(), String> {
641:     let requested_port = crate::port_flag::parse("serve", options.flags.get("port").map(String::as_str))?;
642:     let bind = release_bind(options)?;
643:     let capacity = crate::host_limits::Capacity::from_options(&options.flags, |name| {
644:         std::env::var(name).ok()
645:     })?;
646:     let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
647:     crate::host_limits::initialize();
648:     let owner = crate::store_owner::host(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))?;
649:     owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
650:     let current = current(&dir, &owner)?.ok_or("no current release; deploy first")?;
651:     let release = Project {
652:         root: dir.join("releases").join(current.to_string())
653:             .canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
654:     };
655:     crate::release_files::link_store(&release.root)?;

snapback4/NOT-DOING.md
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A si

codex
Two material concerns remain at `67b605c576745d0afdd0f40a302ec40022447ab7`.

1. **P2 — Write payload columns escape the conservative operation footprint.** In [result_domains.rs:11](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-rel5/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/result_domains.rs:11), traversal collects `Field.name` and `field` selectors but ignores write payload keys encoded as `Expr::Object`. For example:

   ```quarry
   table items:
     n: int 0..10
     public
     insert <- allow
   mutation add(n: int):
     insert items {n}
     return true
   ```

   Changing the column to `int 0..20` changes which calls succeed, yet the footprint contains no `items.n`: the payload stores `"n"` as an object key, and the inferred result is `Bool`. The cached compiler probe compiled both versions without diagnostics and returned an empty compatibility report, corroborating the fixed-source traversal. The write lowering is visible in [program.rs:1150](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-rel5/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:1150).

   Include written column names in the conservative footprint and add a regression whose operation returns a scalar after writing. This requires structural write coverage, without requiring exact result lineage.

2. **P2 — Live rollback skips the new operation-contract comparison.** In [releases.rs:528](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-rel5/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/releases.rs:528), only rollback with `--check` enters `release_preview`, where the comparison runs. Ordinary rollback proceeds directly to activation; `check_change` reports storage compatibility only. Consequently, an operation-only rollback can remove an endpoint or reject newer callers’ arguments without any `INPUT BREAK` warning, although rollback preview identifies it.

   Run the operation comparison before ordinary rollback activation and test the warning on both cold and hot rollback. LLP 3000.000.000.000 §11 explicitly includes rollback alongside its check path.

The source-custody changes address the reviewed in-flight deletion race. I found no further material concern in retained-id reactivation, shared restoration intent, generated requiredness, or optional-argument availability.

Review remained read-only. The isolated probe used cached compiler libraries; it was corroborating evidence, not fresh-build qualification. No Cargo commands or whole suites ran.

VERDICT: NOT READY
